Transfer of homological objects in exact categories via adjoint triples. Applications to functor categories Thanks: 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification. 18G35, 18G25, 18E99.
Sergio Estrada, Manuel Cortés-Izurdiaga, Sİnem Odabaşi

Abstract
. For a given family { ( q i , t i , p i ) } i ∈ I \{({\rm q}_{i},{\rm t}_{i},{\rm p_{i}})\}_{i\in I} of adjoint triples between exact categories 𝒞 \mathcal{C} or 𝒟 \mathcal{D} , we show that any cotorsion pair in 𝒞 \mathcal{C} and 𝒟 \mathcal{D} yield two canonical cotorsion pairs providing a concrete description of objects without using any injectives/projectives object hypothesis. We firstly apply this result for the evaluation functor on the functor category Add ​ ( 𝒜 , R ​ -Mod ) \mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod}) equipped with an exact structure ℰ \mathcal{E} . Under mild conditions on 𝒜 \mathcal{A} , we introduce the stalk functor at any object of 𝒜 \mathcal{A} , and subsequently, we investigate cotorsion pairs induced by stalk functors. Finally, we use them to present an intrinsic characterization of projective/injective objects in ( Add ​ ( 𝒜 , R ​ -Mod ) ; ℰ ) (\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod});\mathcal{E}) .



Introduction

The lifting of particular homological objects to functor categories has been of interest for a long time as it provides a kind of control for globally defined objects through their local information. The emblematic examples come up in the category of chain complexes. The author in
[
Gil04
]
shows that a complete hereditary cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
in
R\mbox{-Mod}
, the category of left
R
-modules, induces a pair of hereditary complete cotorsion pairs
(\mbox{dg}\ \tilde{\mathcal{F}},\tilde{\mathcal{G}})
and
(\tilde{\mathcal{F}},\mbox{dg}\ \tilde{\mathcal{G}})
in
\mathbf{C}(R)
, the category of chain complexes of left
R
-modules, and provides an intrinsic characterization of chain complexes in
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
. Combining it with the well-known result in
[
Hov02
]
, those induced cotorsion pairs yield a model structure on
\mathbf{C}(R)
whose homotopy category is just the derived category
\mathbf{D}(R)
. There are several advantages of this lifting mechanism. Firstly, it generates several non-trivial model structures on
\mathbf{C}(R)
, as well as recovering the well-known injective and projective ones. Secondly, the classes
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
are mostly the classes of categorically defined homological objects in
\mathbf{C}(R)
such as projective, injective, flat and absolutely pure chain complexes. Those results have been generalized to a category of chain complexes
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})
over a sufficiently nice exact category
\mathcal{C}
; see
[
Sto14
, Section 7]
. In the recent work
[
HJ22
]
, the authors focus on the problem of how to obtain projective and injective model structures on
k\mbox{-Lin}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
, the category of
R
-module valued
k
-linear functors on a more general
k
-linear category
\mathcal{A}
, where
k
is a commutative ring and
R
is a
k
-algebra.

Several authors have applied a similar lifting process to modules over a formal triangular matrix ring
L:=\begin{bmatrix}T&0\\
N&S\end{bmatrix}
. For instance, the author in
[
Mao20
]
lifts two cotorsion pairs
(\mathcal{F}_{T},\mathcal{G}_{T})
and
(\mathcal{F}_{S},\mathcal{G}_{S})
in
T\mbox{-Mod}
and
S\mbox{-Mod}
, respectively, to several cotorsion pairs in
L\mbox{-Mod}
providing an explicit description of their modules. As a particular case, the trivial cotorsion pairs
(\mathcal{F}_{T},\mathcal{G}_{T})
and
(\mathcal{F}_{S},\mathcal{G}_{S})
(i.e., projective/injective modules) lead to the trivial cotorsion pairs in
L\mbox{-Mod}
. In this case, the characterization of projective/injective left
L
-modules is the same as in
[
HV99
]
and
[
HV00
]
. The subtle difference with the case of chain complexes is that the classes used to lift come from the module categories over two rings
T
and
S
. However, the standard tool used in both cases is the adjoint functors. So
this paper aims to provide a systematic study of how to lift cotorsion pairs through general adjoint functors between exact categories with applications in functor categories so that it covers the categories of chain complexes and modules over a formal triangular matrix ring. Specifically, we give a very intrinsic characterization of projective and injective additive functors under mild conditions.

The paper starts by proposing a general question: For a given integer
n\geq 1
, and a pair
({\rm q},{\rm t})
of adjoint functors between exact categories
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, under which conditions does there exist a canonical natural isomorphism
{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),-)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm t}(-))}
? Of course, this isomorphism is immediate when both functors are exact between exact categories. However, this is not the case in most situations of interest (for instance, see Example
2.13
). In Section 2, we address this question in full generality, fixing one of the objects in components, that is, for given objects
C\in\mathbf{C}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}
, to guarantee natural isomorphisms

(
\ast
)

{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),-)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(C,{\rm t}(-))}\quad\textrm{ and }\quad{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),D)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm t}(D))}.

The case
n=1
is fully answered in Proposition
2.10
. For it, we introduce two important notions:
{\rm q}
-flat and
{\rm t}
-coflat objects (see Definition
2.6
). These notions are crucial since they help remove the hypothesis of having enough projective/injective objects required in most situations with similar constructions such as
[
HJ19
]
and
[
Mao20
]
(see also Remark
3.2
). We also show that the notion of acyclicity for chain complexes over any exact category can be expressed in terms of (co)-flatness to specific functors (see Example
2.13
).

When working with a general
n\geq 1
, lengthy technical conditions will be required if one aims to avoid projective/injective objects. Instead, we firstly rethink the problem (

∗

) not for fixed objects, but for fixed classes
\mathcal{F}\subseteq\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{G}\subseteq\mathcal{D}
. So under conditions given in Lemma
2.12
, we prove that there exist the following natural isomorphisms

{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(\mathcal{F}),-)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(\mathcal{F},{\rm t}(-))}\quad\textrm{ and }\quad{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),\mathcal{G})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm t}(\mathcal{G}))}.

This result is meritorious on its own. The classic example representing this phenomenon is the adjoint triple induced from sphere chain complexes and acyclic chain complexes (see Example
2.13
). Lemma
2.12
also provides us the tool of the relatively deriving for functors
{\rm q}
and
{\rm t}
to answer the question (

∗

) for any
n\geq 1
(see Proposition
2.15
).

In Section 3, we apply results from Section 2 for the lifting problem of cotorsion pairs through adjoint functors. Corollary
3.3
, a special case of Theorem
3.1
, is the main form of the situations of interest, and is used frequently in the rest of the paper. To explain, if there is a family
\{({\rm q}_{i},{\rm t}_{i},{\rm p_{i}})\}_{i\in I}
of adjoint triples between exact categories

3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 90.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 42.56886pt\raise-5.65277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.65279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm t}_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

then any cotorsion pairs
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
and
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively, yield two canonical cotorsion pairs

({}^{\perp}({\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp}),{\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp})\quad\textrm{ and }\quad({}^{\perp}{\rm p}_{*}(\mathcal{G}),({}^{\perp}{\rm p}_{*}(\mathcal{G}))^{\perp})

({}^{\perp}({\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{\perp}),{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{\perp})\quad\textrm{ and }\quad({}^{\perp}{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}),({}^{\perp}{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))^{\perp})

in
\mathcal{D}
and
\mathcal{C}
, respectively. In Corollary
3.3
, we provide a concrete description of objects in
{\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp}
,
{}^{\perp}{\rm p}_{*}(\mathcal{G})
,
{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{\perp}
and
{}^{\perp}{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
without using any injectives/projectives objects. Applications of this result are presented in the next sections.

Starting from Section 4, we focus on
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
, the category of
R
-module-valued additive functors on an
R
-
R
-bimodule enriched small category
\mathcal{A}
(see Hypothesis
4.3
). Our approach is based on consideration of module categories attached locally to
\mathcal{A}
, that is, the module category over the endomorphism ring
R_{A}:=\mbox{End}_{\mathcal{A}}(A)
of any object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
. We assume that the product category
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
is equipped with a fixed exact sructure
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}_{A}
, and we consider a cotorsion pair
(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
.

In Section 4, we also study the adjoint triple induced from the evaluation functor
\operatorname{ev}_{A}:\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
,
\operatorname{ev}_{A}(X):=X(A)
. As
\mathcal{A}
is an
R
-
R
-bimodule enriched category,
\operatorname{ev}_{A}
factors through
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
. So the fixed exact sructure
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}_{A}
on the product category
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
induces an exact structure
\mathcal{E}
on
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
defined as follows:
\mathbb{E}\in\mathcal{E}
if and only in
\operatorname{ev}_{A}(\mathbb{E})\in\mathcal{E}_{A}
for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
(see Hypothesis
4.10
). So it makes the functor
{\operatorname{ev}:=(\operatorname{ev}_{A})_{A\in\mathcal{A}}:\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\longrightarrow\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}}
an exact functor. Applying Corollary
3.3
for the adjoint triple
({\rm q},\operatorname{ev},{\rm p})
, we have :

Proposition
4.12
:
If for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
, any object in
\mathcal{F}_{A}
and
\mathcal{G}_{A}
is
{\rm q}_{A}
-flat and
{\rm p}_{A}
-coflat, respectively, then

\displaystyle{\rm q}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\mid\ X(A)\in\mathcal{G}_{A}\textrm{ for every }A\in\mathcal{A}\},

{}^{\perp}{\rm p}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\mid\ X(A)\in\mathcal{F}_{A}\textrm{ for every }A\in\mathcal{A}\}.

In Section 5, we impose further conditions (see Hypothesis
5.1
) on the category
\mathcal{A}
in order to define
{\rm s}_{A}:R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\longrightarrow\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
, the ‘stalk’ functor at an object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
(see (
5.3
) and Proposition
5.4
). Our stalk functor differs subtly from
[
HJ22
, Definition 7.9]
, and the one given in the category of representations of quivers (see Remark
5.5
). This difference provides us an advantage in Section 6 when we characterize projective and injective functors in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
for more general categories
\mathcal{A}
than the ones considered in the literature.

The stalk functor is a generalization of the
n
-sphere chain complex functor (see Example
2.13
) in the category of chain complexes, which is crucial in defining the classes
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}
. In Proposition
5.8
, we show that the stalk functors induce an adjoint triple
({\rm c},{\rm s},{\rm k})
between
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
and
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
. In Proposition
5.18
, we describe
{\rm c}
-flat and
{\rm k}
-coflat functors in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
in terms of specific short exact sequences. This concrete characterization, as well as providing us a better understanding of
{\rm c}
-flat and
{\rm k}
-coflat functors, is the key result in proving Theorem
6.5
. In the case of chain complexes, the short exact sequences given in Proposition
5.18
correspond to the canonical short exact sequences

{\rm c}_{i+1}(X)\longrightarrow X_{i}\longrightarrow{\rm c}_{i+1}(X)

{\rm k}_{i}(X)\longrightarrow X_{i}\longrightarrow{\rm k}_{i-1}(X)

(for functors
{\rm c}_{i}
and
{\rm k}_{i}
on chain complexes, see Example
2.13
). Using Corollary
3.3
and Proposition
5.18
, we obtain the following result.

Theorem
5.19
:
If
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A}
and
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A}
are a generating and cogenerating classes in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, respectively, then

\displaystyle{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\biggl\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm k}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{G}_{A},X(A)\longrightarrow\ker(\mu_{A,X})\\
\textrm{is an admissible epic in }R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},\ \forall A\in\mathcal{A}\end{array}\biggr\},

{}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})

\displaystyle=\biggl\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm c}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{F}_{A},\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\hookrightarrow X(A)\\
\textrm{is an admissible monic in }R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},\ \forall A\in\mathcal{A}\end{array}\biggr\}.

In Section 6, we focus on two trivial cotorsion pairs

(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}_{A}\mbox{-Proj},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}),\quad\textrm{ and }\quad(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}_{A}\mbox{-Inj})

to provide an intrinsic caracterization of projective and injective objects in the exact category
(\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod});\mathcal{E})
.

Theorem
6.5
:

(i)

If Condition
6.1
is satisfied, then

\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Proj}={}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}).

(ii)

If Condition
6.2
is satisfied, then

\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Inj}={\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod})^{\perp}

It is worth pointing out that Theorem
6.5
provides a characterization of relative projective and injective functors. Moreover, in the absolute exact structure case, it applies to more general categories
\mathcal{A}
than the ones considered in
[
HJ22
, Theorem 7.29]
(see Example
6.4
) and covers both a Morita context ring with zero trace ideals (as a particular case, formal triangular matrix rings) and left/right rooted categories. Therefore, our result extends the results in
[
HV99
]
,
[
HV00
]
and
[
CT13
, Theorem1]
.

1.
Preliminares

In this section, we review certain basic notions and results that are essential for our study.

1.1. Product category.

Let
\{{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a family of additive categories.
The product category
, denoted by
\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
, is the category with objects

\mbox{Ob}({}\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}}_{i}):=\prod_{i\in I}\mbox{Ob}({\mathcal{C}}_{i}),

and morphisms

\operatorname{Hom}_{\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}}_{i}}((C_{i})_{i\in I},(C^{\prime}_{i})_{i\in I}):=\prod_{i\in I}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{i}}(C_{i},C_{i}^{\prime}).

For any
i\in I
,
\pi_{i}
and
\iota_{i}
denote the canonical
i
th projection and inclusion functors, respectively,

\pi_{i}:\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{i},

\iota_{i}:{\mathcal{C}}_{i}\longrightarrow\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}}_{i}.

1.2. Exact categories.

An
exact structure on an additive category
\mathcal{C}
is an isomorphism-closed collection
\mathcal{E}
of distinguished kernel-cokernel pairs
(i,p)
in
\mathcal{C}

\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.48206pt\raise 5.31596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.31596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.71954pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

which are called
admissible monic
and
admissible epic
of
\mathbb{E}
, respectively,
satisfying:

(i)

For every object
A\in\mathcal{C},
the identity morphism
\operatorname{id}_{A}
is both an admissible monic and an admissible epic;

(ii)

Admissible monics and admissible epics are closed under compositions;

(iii)

The class
\mathcal{E}
is closed under pushout and pullback along a morphism in
\mathcal{C}
.

\mathcal{C}
equipped with an exact structure
\mathcal{E}
, denoted by
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
, is called an
exact category
. A kernel-cokernel pair
(i,p)
, or
\mathbb{E}
, in
\mathcal{E}
is called a
short exact sequence
in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
.

A sequence in
\mathcal{C}
of the form

\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.28012pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.28012pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.78012pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 111.78012pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 154.35306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 154.35306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

is said to be
exact
if there exist short exact sequences
\mathbb{E}_{i}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.43547pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.43547pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.43549pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.43547pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.43547pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.85155pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.85155pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z_{i-1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
,
1\leq i\leq n
, with
Z_{0}=C
and
Z_{n}=A
in such a way that the morphism
{B_{i+1}\longrightarrow B_{i}}
is factorized as
\textstyle{B_{i+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{i}}
for every
1\leq i\leq n-1.
In other words,
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}_{n}\circ\cdots\circ\mathbb{E}_{1}
. We refer to
[
Buh10
]
for a detailed treatement on exact categories.

We recall the following result which will be used in Section 4.

1.3. Proposition.

[
Buh10
, Corollary 2.14]
. Let
({\mathcal{C}};\mathcal{E})
be an exact category. For any morphism
f:A\rightarrow B
in
{\mathcal{C}}
, the following holds:

(i)

If
g:C\rightarrow B
is an admissible epic in
({\mathcal{C}};\mathcal{E})
, then so is the induced morphism
(f,g):A\oplus C\rightarrow B
.

(ii)

If
f:A\rightarrow C
is an admissible monic in
({\mathcal{C}};\mathcal{E})
, then so is the induced morphism
(f,g):A\rightarrow B\oplus C
.

1.4. Cotorsion pairs.

For any integer
n\geq 1
, the exact structure
\mathcal{E}
on
\mathcal{C}
leads to the so-called
n
th Yoneda extension functor

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}:\mathcal{C}^{\textrm{op}}\times\mathcal{C}\to\operatorname{Ab},

where

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(C,A)
denotes the abelian group of equivalence classes of all exact sequences in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
of the form

\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.28012pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.28012pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 111.78012pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 111.78012pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 154.35306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 154.35306pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

For given morphisms
i:C^{\prime}\to C
,
j:A\to A^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{C}
, the morphisms

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(i,A):\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(C,A)\to\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(C^{\prime},A),\quad\mathbb{E}\leadsto\mathbb{E}i

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(C,j):\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(C,A)\to\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(C,A^{\prime}),\quad\mathbb{E}\leadsto j\mathbb{E},

where
\mathbb{E}i
and
j\mathbb{E}
denote the pullback and pushout of
\mathbb{E}
along
i
and
j
, respectively. So we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(i,j)=\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(i,A^{\prime})\circ\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(C,j)\cong\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{E}}^{n}(C^{\prime},j)\circ\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{E}}^{n}(i,A),

that is,
(j\mathbb{E})i\equiv j(\mathbb{E}i)
.

For a given class
\mathcal{S}
of objects in
\mathcal{C}
, we let

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\{A\in\mathcal{C}\mid\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(S,A)=0,\textrm{ for every }S\in\mathcal{S}\};

{}^{\perp}\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle=\{A\in\mathcal{C}\mid\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(A,S)=0,\textrm{ for every }S\in\mathcal{S}\}.

A pair
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
of classes of objects in
\mathcal{C}
is said to be
cotorsion pair
if
\mathcal{F}^{\perp}=\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{F}={}^{\perp}\mathcal{G}
. It is said to be
hereditary
if
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}}(F,G)=0
for every
F\in\mathcal{F}
,
G\in\mathcal{G}
and
n\geq 1
.

For a class
\mathcal{S}
of objects in
\mathcal{C}
, the cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
is said to be
generated by

\mathcal{S}
if
\mathcal{S}^{\perp}=\mathcal{G}
; is said to be
cogenerated by

\mathcal{S}
if
\mathcal{F}={}^{\perp}\mathcal{S}
.

The cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
is said to
have enough injectives
if for every object
A
of
\mathcal{C}
, there exists a short exact sequence in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
of the form

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}

with
G\in\mathcal{G}
and
F\in\mathcal{F}
; it is said to

have enough projectives
if for every object
A
of
\mathcal{C}
, there exists a short exact sequence in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
of the form

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.33401pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.33401pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{G^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.33403pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.33401pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 32.33401pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.95901pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 72.95901pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
G^{\prime}\in\mathcal{G}
and
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}
; it is said to be

complete
if it has enough projectives and injectives.

1.5. Projective and injective objects.

An object
P
in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
is said to be
projective
if for every short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}
in
\mathcal{E}
, the sequence
\operatorname{Hom}(P,\mathbb{E})
of abelian groups is exact. The class of all projective objects in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
is denoted by
\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Proj}
.

(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
is said to have
enough projectives
if
(\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Proj},\mathcal{C})
is a complete cotorsion pair, equivalently, for every object
A
in
\mathcal{C}
there exists an admissible epic
P\twoheadrightarrow A
with
P\in\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Proj}
.

Dually, an
injective object
in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
is defined, and the class of injective objects in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
is denoted by
\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Inj}
.
\mathcal{C}
is said to have
enough injectives
if for every object
A
in
\mathcal{C}
there exists an admissible monic
A\hookrightarrow E
with
E\in\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Inj}
.

1.6. Left and right derived functors.

[
Buh10
, Section 10, Remark 12.12]
Let
\mathcal{F}
be an extension closed class of objects in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
satisfying

(i)

\mathcal{F}
is a
generating class
, that is, for every object
A
in
\mathcal{C}
, there exists an admissible epic
F\twoheadrightarrow A
with
F
in
\mathcal{F}
;

(ii)

\mathcal{F}
is closed under kernels of admissible epics, that is, if
\textstyle{F^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{\prime\prime}}
is a short exact sequence in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
with objects
F,F^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathcal{F}
, then so is
F^{\prime}
.

So for any object
A
in
\mathcal{C}
there exists an exact sequence in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
of the form

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A}

with
F_{i}\in\mathcal{F}
for every
i\geq 0
.
The deleted chain complex

F_{*}^{A}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.05557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.05557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 115.36115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 115.36115pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

is called a
\mathcal{F}
-
resolution
of
A
. If an additive functor
\rm p:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
, where
\mathcal{D}
is an abelian category, preserves short exact sequences in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})
with objects in
\mathcal{F}
, then for any integer
i\geq 0
, the so-called
i
th
left derived functor of
\rm p
relative to
\mathcal{F}
, denoted by
\rm L_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}p
, exists and is the functor from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
defined by
\rm{L_{i}^{\mathcal{F}}p}(A):=\operatorname{H}_{i}({\rm p}(F^{A}_{*}))
, the
i
th homology of the chain complex
{\rm p}(F_{*}^{A})
. It is well known that
any short exact sequence in
(\mathcal{C};\mathcal{E})

\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.48206pt\raise 5.31596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.31596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.71954pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

induces the following long exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm L_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}p}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm L_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}p}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm L_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}p}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm L_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}p}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm L_{0}^{\mathcal{F}}p}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

and if
{\rm p}
is right exact, then
{\rm L_{0}}^{\mathcal{F}}{\rm p}\cong\rm p
.

If
\mathcal{F}
satisfies dual properties, then every object in
\mathcal{C}
has a
\mathcal{F}
-
coresolution
, and the
i
th
right derived functor
, denoted by
{\rm R^{i}_{\mathcal{F}}p}:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
, of
{\rm p}
exists and is defined dually.

1.7.

Returning to the product categories, for a family
\{({\mathcal{C}}_{i};\mathcal{E}_{i})\}_{i\in I}
of exact categories,
the product exact structure

\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i}
on the product category
\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
consists of
I
-tuples
(\mathbb{E}_{i})_{i\in I}
of short exact sequences
\mathbb{E}_{i}
in
(\mathcal{C}_{i};\mathcal{E}_{i})
.
So we have

(1.7.1)

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i}}((C_{i})_{i\in I},(A_{i})_{i\in I}):=\prod_{i\in I}\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{\mathcal{E}_{i}}(C_{i},A_{i}).

The following is an easy observation, but for the reader’s convenience, we provide its proof.

1.8. Lemma.

Any (hereditary) cotorsion pair in
(\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}}_{i};\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i})
is of the form

(\ {}\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i},\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{G}_{i}),

where
(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G}_{i})
is a (hereditary) cotorsion pair in
(\mathcal{C}_{i};\mathcal{E}_{i})
for every
i\in I
.

Proof.

Let
\{(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G}_{i})\}_{i\in I}
be a family of (hereditary) cotorsion pairs in
(\mathcal{C}_{i};\mathcal{E}_{i})
. From (
1.7.1
), we already have

\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i}\subseteq{}^{\perp}(\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{G}_{i})
and

\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{G}_{i}\subseteq\ (\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i})^{\perp}
. Let
(C_{i})_{i\in I}
be an object in

{}^{\perp}(\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{G}_{i})
. It is clear that for any object
G_{i_{0}}
in
\mathcal{G}_{i}
,
{\iota_{i_{0}}(G_{i_{0}})\in\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{G}_{i}}
, and by assumption,
we have

0=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i}}(\ (C_{i})_{i\in I},\iota_{i_{0}}(G_{\iota_{0}}))=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{i_{0}}}(C_{i_{0}},G_{i_{0}}).

Therefore, for every
i\in I
,
C_{i}\in\ ^{\perp}\mathcal{G}_{i}=\mathcal{F}_{i}.
Similarly, we have
{(\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{F}_{i})^{\perp}\subseteq\ \prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{G}_{i}}
.

Now, suppose that
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
is a cotorsion pair in
(\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{C}}_{i};\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i})
. For every
i\in I
, we let
\mathcal{F}_{i}:=\pi_{i}(\mathcal{F})
and
\mathcal{G}_{i}:=\pi_{i}(\mathcal{G})
. From (
1.7.1
), it is easy to observe that
\mathcal{F}_{i}\subseteq{}^{\perp}\mathcal{G}_{i}
and
\mathcal{G}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\perp}
for every
i\in I
. Now, let
C_{i_{0}}\in{}^{\perp}\mathcal{G}_{i_{0}}
for some
i_{0}\in I
. For every
(G_{i})_{i\in I}\in\mathcal{G}
, we have
G_{i_{0}}\in\mathcal{G}_{i_{0}}
and

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\prod_{i\in I}\mathcal{E}_{i}}(\iota_{i_{0}}(C_{i_{0}}),(G_{i})_{i\in I})=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{i_{0}}}(C_{i_{0}},G_{i_{0}})=0,

therefore,
\iota_{i_{0}}(C_{i_{0}})\in\mathcal{F}
. It implies that
C_{i_{0}}=\pi_{i_{0}}(\iota_{i_{0}}(C_{i_{0}}))\in\mathcal{F}_{i_{0}}
. Similarly, we have
\mathcal{G}_{i}=\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\perp}
for every
i\in I
.

The hereditary condition follows easily.
∎

2.
\operatorname{Ext}
isomorphisms.

Throughout,
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
denote weakly idempotent complete exact categories equipped with exact structures
\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, respectively. So we omit the subscript in the corresponding
n
th Yoneda extension functors. We also fix an adjoint pair
({\rm q},{\rm t})
of functors between
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.6146pt\raise 15.18752pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm q}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 90.6157pt\raise 2.60219pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 60.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 90.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.15279pt\raise-15.15279pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rm t}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 6.61276pt\raise-2.53061pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We let
\eta:\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}\Rightarrow{\rm tq}
and
\xi:{\rm qt}\Rightarrow\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{D}}
denote the unit and the counit of the adjunction
({\rm q},{\rm t})
. It is well known that
{\rm q}
preserves all colimits while
{\rm t}
preserves all limits.

In this section, we investigate sufficient and necessary conditions for which the functors
{\rm q}
and
{\rm t}
interact together inside the functor
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}
in the same way as they do inside of the
\operatorname{Hom}
functor.
The following presents a more general form of the result
[
HJ19
, Lemma 5.1]
.

2.1. Lemma.

For any objects
C
and
D
in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively, and an integer
n\geq 1
, the following hold:

(i)

If
{\rm t}
preserves exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),D)
, then there exists a canonical morphism

\Theta_{CD}^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),D)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(C,{\rm t}(D)).

If
n=1
, then
\Theta_{CD}^{1}
is a monomorphism.

(ii)

If
{\rm q}
preserves exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(C,{\rm t}(D))
, then there exists a canonical morphism

\Omega_{CD}^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}(C,{\rm t}(D))\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),D).

If
n=1
, then
\Omega_{CD}^{1}
is a monomorphism.

(iii)

If functors
{\rm q}
and
{\rm t}
satisfy conditions given in (i)-(ii), then

\Theta_{CD}^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),D)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(C,{\rm t}(D))

is an isomorphism with the inverse
\Omega_{CD}^{n}
.

Proof.

(i)

Consider an exact sequence
\mathbb{E}
in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),D)
. By assumption,
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})
is an exact sequence in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(tq(C),{\rm t}(D))
.
Taking the pullback of
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})
along the unit morphism
\eta_{C}:\ C\longrightarrow tq(X)
, we obtain an exact sequence
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C}
in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(C,{\rm t}(D))
together with the pullback diagram
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C}\rightarrow{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})
.

Since taking pullback and
{\rm t}
are additive functors, the assignment

\Theta^{n}_{CD}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(C),D)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(C,{\rm t}(D)),\quad\mathbb{E}\rightsquigarrow{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C}

is well-defined.

Now, we show that
\Theta^{1}_{CD}
is a monomorphism. Suppose that for some short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}
in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(C),D)
, the short exact sequence
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C}
is trivial in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(C,{\rm t}(D))
. As
{\rm q}
is additive,
{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C})
is trivial, as well. On the other hand, we consider the composition of the following canonical morphisms between sequences

(2.1.1)

{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C})\longrightarrow qt(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow\mathbb{E},

where
qt(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}
is the morphism induced from the counit natural transformation
\xi
. The last column of the composition given in (
2.1.1
) is the composition

\xi_{{\rm q}(C)}\circ{\rm q}(\eta_{C}):{\rm q}(C)\longrightarrow qtq(C)\longrightarrow{\rm q}(C),

which is identity. Hence, the short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}
is in fact a pushout of the trivial short exact sequence
{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C})
. As a consequence,
\mathbb{E}
is a trivial short exact sequence.

(ii)

Similar to the proof of the first statement.

(iii)

From (i) and (ii), we have assignments
\Theta_{CD}^{n}
and
\Omega_{CD}^{n}
. We claim that

\Omega_{CD}^{n}\circ\Theta_{CD}^{n}=\operatorname{id}\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\Theta_{CD}^{n}\circ\Omega_{CD}^{n}=\operatorname{id}.

By duality of arguments, we only show the equality
\Theta_{CD}^{n}\circ\Omega_{CD}^{n}=\operatorname{id}
.

Let
\mathbb{E}
be any exact sequence in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),D)
. By construction, we have
\Theta^{n}_{CD}(\mathbb{E})={\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C}
together with the pullback diagram
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C}\longrightarrow{\rm t}(\mathbb{E})
. Applying
{\rm q}
to the aforementioned pullback diagram, we still have a commutative diagram
{{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C})\longrightarrow qt(\mathbb{E})}
with the rightest vertical arrow
{\rm q}({\rm t}(\eta_{C}))
. By assumption,
{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{C})
is an exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
. Composing it with the counit morphism, we have a commutative diagram with canonical morphisms

{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{X})\longrightarrow qt(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow\mathbb{E},

The last column of the composition is the composition

\xi_{p(C)}\circ{\rm q}(\eta_{C}):{\rm q}(C)\longrightarrow qtq(C)\longrightarrow{\rm q}(C),

which is identity. And the left vertical map is
\xi_{D}
. By universal property of pushouts, the commutative diagram
{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{X})\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}
is factorized as

{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{X})\longrightarrow\xi_{D}{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{X})\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}

where both
\Omega^{n}_{CD}\Theta^{n}_{CD}(\mathbb{E})=\xi_{D}{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E})\eta_{X})
and
\mathbb{E}
belongs to
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),D)
. So
\Omega^{n}_{CD}\Theta^{n}_{CD}(\mathbb{E})\equiv\mathbb{E}
.

∎

As an aside, it is easy to verify that if
1\leq m\leq n
and
{\rm t}
preserves exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(C),D)
, then it preserves also exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{m}({\rm q}(C),D)
, and therefore, there exists a morphism
\Theta_{CD}^{m}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{m}({\rm q}(C),D)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{m}(C,{\rm t}(D)).
The dual affirmation is valid for
{\rm q}
.

As pullback and pushout diagrams satisfy the universal property, and the (co)unit of an adjunction is a natural transformation, we have the following result.

2.2. Corollary.

Let
n\geq 1
. We have the following:

(i)

If
{\rm t}
preserves exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),-)
, then
\Theta^{n}:=\{\Theta_{CD}^{n}\}_{C\in\mathcal{C},D\in\mathcal{D}}
is a natural transformation

\Theta^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm t}(-)).

(ii)

If
{\rm q}
preserves exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm t}(-))
, then
\Omega^{n}:=\{\Omega^{n}_{CD}\}_{C\in\mathcal{C},D\in\mathcal{D}}
is a natural transformation

\Omega^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm t}(-))\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),-).

(iii)

If functors
{\rm q}
and
{\rm t}
satisfy conditions given in (i)-(ii), then the natural transformation

\Theta^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm t}(-))

is an isomorphism with the inverse
\Omega^{n}
.

2.3. Remark

The condition that
{\rm t}
preserves exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),-)
for some
n\geq 1
is equivalent to the exactness of
{\rm t}
; see Lemma
2.8
. Therefore, if both functors
{\rm t}
and
{\rm q}
are exact, then the natural transformation

\Theta^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(-),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm t}(-))

is an isomorphism for every
n\geq 1
.

2.4. Example.

We let
(\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C});\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{E}))
denote the category of chain complexes of objects in
\mathcal{C}
equipped with the degrewise exact structure. For every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is an adjoint triple
(\operatorname{D}^{i},\operatorname{ev}_{i},\operatorname{D}^{i+1})

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.03761pt\raise 26.17511pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.17508pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{D}^{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 93.40378pt\raise 7.99814pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.76671pt\raise-26.17561pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.17558pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{D}^{i+1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 93.40378pt\raise-7.99814pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 60.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 90.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.16953pt\raise-5.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ev}_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
\operatorname{ev}_{i}
denotes the
i
th evaluation functor, and
\operatorname{D}^{i}(C)
is the chain complex with
C
at
i
th and
(i-1)
th positions, and
0
elsewhere. For every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the functors
\operatorname{D}^{i}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{i}
are known to be exact, and therefore, we have natural isomorphism

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(\operatorname{D}^{i}(-),-)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,\operatorname{ev}_{i}(-))\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(\operatorname{ev}_{i}(-),-)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,\operatorname{D}^{i+1}(-))

for every
n\geq 1
.

2.5. Example.

Let
X
be a semi-separated scheme. For any affine subscheme
{\iota:U\hookrightarrow X}
, we have the adjoint pair
(\operatorname{res},\iota_{*})
of functors

\textstyle{\mathfrak{Qco}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Qco}(U)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{*}}

between categories of quasi-coherent sheaves on
X
and
U
.
Both the restriction
\operatorname{res}
and the direct image functor
\iota_{*}
are exact, and therefore, for every
n\geq 1

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(\operatorname{res}(-),-)\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,\iota_{*}(-)).

From now on, even though we present statements together with their dual versions, we only prove the first statement as their arguments can be dualized easily. We also use notations presented in Lemma (
2.1
).

Now we investigate conditions for which
\Theta_{C}^{n}
and
\Omega_{D}^{n}
are isomorphisms for fixed objects
C\in\mathcal{C}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}
. As a first step, we study the case
n=1
. For it, we introduce the following definition.

2.6. Definition.

We call an object
C
in
\mathcal{C}

\rm q
-
flat
if every short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
ending with
C
remains exact under
\rm q
. Dually, a
\rm t
-
coflat
object in
\mathcal{D}
is defined.

2.7. Remark.

In
[
Pre10
]
, for given two proper classes
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{R}
in an abelian category
\mathcal{B}
, the notions of
\mathcal{P}
-
\mathcal{R}
-flat and divisible object have been introduced. Namely, an object
B
in
\mathcal{B}
is called
\mathcal{P}
-
\mathcal{R}
-flat
if every short exact sequence
in
\mathcal{P}
ending at
B
belongs to
\mathcal{R}
.
\mathcal{P}
-
\mathcal{R}
divisibles are defined dually. In our case, since
{\rm q}
is a left adjoint functor, it can be easily proved that the class
\overline{\mathcal{E}}
consisting of short exact sequences in
\mathcal{E}
which remains exact under
{\rm q}
is an exact substructure of
\mathcal{E}
. So an object
C
in
\mathcal{C}
is
{\rm q}
-flat if and only if it is
\mathcal{E}
-
\overline{\mathcal{E}}
-flat in the sense of
[
Pre10
]
. The dual statment holds for
{p}
-coflat and divisible objects.

The following lemma will be used for proving Proposition
2.10
.

2.8. Lemma.

For given objects
C\in\mathcal{C}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}
, the following hold:

(i)

The functor
{\rm q}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(C,{\rm t}(-))
if and only if
C
is
\rm q
-
flat
.

(ii)

The functor
{\rm t}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(-),D)
if and only if
D
is
\rm t
-
coflat
.

Proof.

The “if” condition of the statement (i) is immediate. For the ”only if” part, suppose that
{\rm q}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(C,{\rm t}(-))
. Consider a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}

\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.48206pt\raise 5.31596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.31596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.71954pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

By taking pushout of
\mathbb{E}
along the unit morphism
\eta_{A}
, we have a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}

\eta_{A}\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.22224pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.22224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm tq}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.22224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.22224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 80.11461pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 80.11461pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

together with the pushout diagram
\mathbb{E}\longrightarrow\eta_{A}\mathbb{E}
. By assumption,
{\rm q}(\eta_{A}\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
. Taking pushout of
{\rm q}(\eta_{A}\mathbb{E})
along the morphism
\xi_{{\rm q}(A)}
, we obtain a short exact sequence
\xi_{{\rm q}(A)}{\rm q}(\eta_{A}\mathbb{E})
together with the commutative diagram

{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow{\rm q}(\eta_{A}\mathbb{E})\rightarrow\xi_{{\rm q}(A)}{\rm q}(\eta_{A}\mathbb{E})

\textstyle{{\rm q}(\mathbb{E}):\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm q}(i)}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm q}(p)}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\xi_{{\rm q}(A)}{\rm q}(\eta_{A}\mathbb{E}):}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(C)}

Since
{\rm q}
preserves cokernels,
{\rm q}(p)
is the cokernel of
{\rm q}(i)
, and by
[
Buh10
, Proposition 2.1.6]
,
{\rm q}(i)
is an admissible monic in
\mathcal{D}
, and therefore,
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
.

∎

The following lemma, which will be used to prove Theorem
3.1
, illustrates that (co)generating classes are sufficient for showing (co)flatness of an object.

2.9. Lemma.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be classes of objects in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively. The followings hold:

(i)

If
\mathcal{G}
is a cogenerating class in
\mathcal{D}
, and
{\rm q}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(C,{\rm t}(\mathcal{G}))
, then
C
is a
{\rm q}
-flat object.

(ii)

If
\mathcal{F}
is a generating class in
\mathcal{C}
, and
{\rm t}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(\mathcal{F}),D)
, then
D
is a
\rm t
-coflat object.

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.4868pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.4868pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.4868pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.4868pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.4868pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.15483pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.15483pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
be a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
. By assumption, there exists an admissible monic
f:{\rm q}(C^{\prime\prime})\hookrightarrow G
in
\mathcal{D}
with
G\in\mathcal{G}
. Let
\overline{f}:={\rm t}(f)\circ\eta_{C^{\prime\prime}}:C^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow{\rm t}(G)
.
Consider the pushout diagram
\mathbb{E}\longrightarrow\overline{f}\mathbb{E}
.
\overline{f}\mathbb{E}
is a short exact sequence in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(C,{\rm t}(G))
, and by assumption,
{\rm q}(\overline{f}\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
. Again, taking pushout of the short exact sequence
{\rm q}(\overline{f}\mathbb{E})
along the counit morphism
\xi_{G}:{\rm qt}(G)\rightarrow G
, we obtain a short exact sequence
\xi_{G}{\rm q}(\overline{f}\mathbb{E})
. Consider the natural composition of sequences

{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow{\rm q}(\overline{f}\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow\xi_{G}{\rm q}(\overline{f}\mathbb{E}).

Note that the morphism on the most left column of the above composite is
\xi_{G}\circ{{\rm q}(\overline{f})=f}
, which is an admissible monic in
\mathcal{D}
. Since
{\rm q}
preserves cokernels, the morphism
{{\rm q}(C^{\prime})\rightarrow{\rm q}(C)}
is the cokernel of the morphism
{\rm q}(C^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow{\rm q}(C^{\prime})
, and by
[
Buh10
, Proposition 2.16]
,
{\rm q}(C^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow{\rm q}(C^{\prime})
is an admissible monic in
\mathcal{D}
, and therefore,
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
.
∎

2.10. Proposition.

For given objects
C\in\mathcal{C}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}
, the following hold:

(i)

Suppose that
{\rm t}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(C),-)
. Then the following are equivalent:

(a)

The natural transformation

\Theta_{C}^{1}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(C),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(C,{\rm t}(-)),

is an isomorphism.

(b)

C
is a
{\rm q}
-flat object in
\mathcal{C}
.

(ii)

Suppose that
{\rm q}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,{\rm t}(D))
. Then the following are equivalent:

(a)

The natural transformation

\Omega_{D}^{1}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,{\rm t}(D))\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(-),D),

is an isomorphism.

(b)

D
is a
{\rm t}
-coflat object in
\mathcal{D}
.

Proof.

The implication (b)
\Rightarrow
(a) in (i) follows from Lemma
2.1
-(iii) and Lemma
2.8
-(i).
Now suppose that the natural transformation
\Theta_{C}^{1}
is an isomorphism. Consider a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}

\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.48206pt\raise 5.31596pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.31596pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.71954pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

By taking pushout of
\mathbb{E}
along the unit morphism
\eta_{A}
, we have a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}

\eta_{A}\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.22224pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.22224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm tq}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.22226pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.22224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.22224pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 80.11461pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 80.11461pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

together with the pushout diagram
\mathbb{E}\longrightarrow\eta_{A}\mathbb{E}
. By assumption, there exists a short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.2778pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.2778pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm q}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.2778pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.2778pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.2778pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.34723pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 76.34723pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm q}(C)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
in
\mathcal{D}
such that
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime})\eta_{C}\equiv\eta_{A}\mathbb{E}
. Note that, by assumption,
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
. Applying
{\rm q}
, we have a commutative diagram
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow{\rm q}(\eta_{A}\mathbb{E})\equiv{\rm q}({\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime})\eta_{C})\rightarrow qt(\mathbb{E}^{\prime}).

Composing it with the canonical diagram
qt(\mathbb{E}^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}^{\prime}
induced from the counit natural transformation
\xi
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{\rm q}(\mathbb{E}):\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm q}(i)}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm q}(p)}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}:}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm q}(C)}

Since
{\rm q}
preserves cokernels,
{\rm q}(p)
is the cokernel of
{\rm q}(i)
, and by
[
Buh10
, Proposition 2.1.6]
,
{\rm q}(i)
is an admissible monic in
\mathcal{D}
, and therefore,
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
.
∎

The previous result covers the one proven in
[
AP22
, Theorem 4.2]
for
{\bf I}
-directed colimit functor, as shown in the following example.

2.11. Example.

Let
\mbox{Fun}({\bf I},\mathcal{C})
be the category of functors from
{\bf I}
to
\mathcal{C}
equipped with the degrewise exact structure
\mbox{Fun}({\bf I},\mathcal{E})
. We let
\kappa_{\bf I}:\ \mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mbox{Fun}({\bf I},\mathcal{C})
denote the constant functor, that is, for a given object
C
in
\mathcal{C}
,
\kappa_{\bf I}(C)(i):=C
and
\kappa_{\bf I}(C)(f):=\mbox{id}_{C}
for every object
i
and a morphism
f
in
{\bf I}
. It is a well-known fact that
\kappa_{\bf I}
is an exact functor, and
\kappa_{\bf I}
has a left adjoint if and only if
\mathcal{C}
has
{\bf I}
-directed colimits. We assume that
\mathcal{C}
has
{\bf I}
-directed colimits. For a given functor
F:{\bf I}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
,
\mbox{colim}_{\bf I}(F)
denotes its colimit. So the canonical natural transformation

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mbox{colim}_{\bf I}(F),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(F,\kappa_{\bf I}(-))

is an isomorphism if and only if for every short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}
in
\mbox{Fun}({\bf I},\mathcal{C})
ending with
F
,
\mbox{colim}_{\bf I}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
, as well. In particular, if
\mathcal{C}
is an abelian category with
{\bf I}
-directed colimits, then the functor of
{\bf I}
-directed colimits in
\mathcal{C}
is exact if and only if
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mbox{colim}_{\bf I}(-),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,\kappa_{\bf I}(-))
is an isomorphism; see
[
AP22
, Theorem 4.2]
.

As a particular case, if
\bf I
is a discrete category, then
\mbox{Fun}({\bf I},\mathcal{C})\cong\prod_{i\in{\bf I}}\mathcal{C}
, and
\mbox{colim}_{\bf I}(-)\cong\oplus_{i\in{\bf I}}
. So
\mathcal{C}
has
{\bf I}
-indexed coproducts if and only if
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\oplus_{\bf I}(-),-)\cong\prod_{i\in{\bf I}}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,-)
.

To obtain similar results for
\Theta^{n}_{C}
and
\Omega^{n}_{D}
for any
n\geq 1
in such generality, we would need to consider some lengthy technical conditions, such as being all syzygies of
C

{\rm q}
-flat. Instead, we prefer to use left and right derived functors (see Proposition
2.15
). For it, we prove the following two lemmas.

2.12. Lemma.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be extension closed-classes of objects in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
satisfying conditions (i)-(ii) and their duals given in (
1.6
), respectively. The followings hold:

(i)

Suppose that
{\rm t}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(\mathcal{F}),-)
.
The functor
{\rm q}
restricted on
\mathcal{F}
is exact if and only if
\Theta_{F}^{n}
is an isomorphism for every
F\in\mathcal{F}
and
n\geq 1
.

(ii)

Suppose that
{\rm q}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,{\rm t}(\mathcal{G}))
.
The functor
{\rm t}
restricted on
{\mathcal{G}}
is exact if and only if
\Omega_{D}^{n}
is an isomorphism for every
G\in\mathcal{G}
and
n\geq 1
.

Proof.

Firstly, note that the functor
{\rm t}
is an exact functor by Lemma
2.9
. So the natural transformation
\Theta_{F}^{n}:\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(F),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(F,{\rm t}(-))
exists for every
F\in\mathcal{F}
and
n\geq 1
.

The ”if” statement now follows from Proposition
2.10
. For the ”only if” part, suppose that
{\rm q}
preserves short exact sequences in
\mathcal{C}
with objects in
\mathcal{F}
. We firstly prove that
{\rm q}
preserves all short exact sequences in
\mathcal{C}
ending with an object in
\mathcal{F}
. Let

\mathbb{E}:\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.75002pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

be a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
with an object
F
in
\mathcal{F}
. By assumption, there exists an admissible epic
F_{0}\twoheadrightarrow B
with
F_{0}\in\mathcal{F}
, which induces the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{\prime}:}
\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{F_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}:}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}

with exact rows, and therefore,
f\mathbb{E}^{\prime}=\mathbb{E}
. Again by assumption,
F_{1}\in\mathcal{F}
, and hence,
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
. Since
{\rm q}
preserves all colimits, it preserves cokernels and pushout diagrams. So we have

{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})\cong{\rm q}(f\mathbb{E}^{\prime})\cong{\rm q}(f){\rm q}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime}),

that is,
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a pushout of
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime})
along
{\rm q}(f)
. It implies that
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
, and by Proposition
2.10
,
\Theta_{F}^{1}
is an isomorphism for every
F
in
\mathcal{F}
.

Now, we prove the statement for
n=2
. The natural transformation

\Theta_{F}^{2}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{2}({\rm q}(F),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{2}(F,{\rm t}(-))

is an epimorphism
for every
F\in\mathcal{F}
. Indeed, by a similar argument just as done above, any exact sequence in
\operatorname{Ext}^{2}(F,{\rm t}(D))
is equivalent to an exact sequence of the form

(2.12.1)

\mathbb{E}:\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.11182pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.11182pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm t}(D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.11183pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.11182pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 118.48682pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.18124pt\raise-24.5518pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 82.33403pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 118.48682pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
