3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 160.79239pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 160.79239pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 41.64653pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 76.18124pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.48683pt\raise-24.81995pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 118.48682pt\raise-6.84671pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 124.6396pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 164.70212pt\raise-31.66666pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

with
F,F_{0},F_{1}\in\mathcal{F}
.
Consider the short exact sequences
\mathbb{E}_{1}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 9.15279pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-9.15279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.1528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 33.15279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 33.15279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.45836pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 75.45836pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
and

\mathbb{E}_{2}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.11182pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.11182pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm t}(D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.11183pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.11182pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.11182pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.18124pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 76.18124pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F_{1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces
in
\mathcal{C}
. Since
\Theta_{F_{1}D}^{1}
is an isomorphism, there exists a short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{2}
in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(F_{1}),D)
such that
\Theta_{F_{1}D}^{1}(\mathbb{E}_{2}^{\prime})={\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{2})\eta_{F_{1}}\equiv\mathbb{E}_{2}
. So we have

\mathbb{E}\equiv\mathbb{E}_{2}\circ\mathbb{E}_{1}\equiv({\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{2})\eta_{F_{1}})\circ\mathbb{E}_{1}\equiv{\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{2})\circ(\eta_{F_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{1}).

Note that
\eta_{F_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{1}
is a short exact sequence in

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(F,{\rm tq}(F_{1}))
.
Similarly, as
\Theta_{F{\rm q}(F_{1})}^{1}
is an isomorphism, there exists a short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{1}
in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(F),{\rm q}(F_{1}))
such that
{\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{1})\eta_{F}\equiv\eta_{F_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{1}
. Let
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}:=\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{2}\circ\mathbb{E}^{\prime}_{1}
. Notice that
\mathbb{E}^{\prime}
is an exact sequence in
\operatorname{Ext}^{2}({\rm q}(F),D)
, and

{\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime})\eta_{F}={\rm t}(\mathbb{E}_{2}^{\prime})\circ({\rm t}(\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\prime})\eta_{F})\equiv{\rm t}(\mathbb{E}_{2}^{\prime})\circ(\eta_{F_{1}}\mathbb{E}_{1})\equiv\mathbb{E}.

Now, we prove that
\Theta_{FD}^{2}
is injective. Let
\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}
be an exact sequence in
\operatorname{Ext}^{2}({\rm q}(F),D)
such that
\Theta_{FD}^{2}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime})={\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime})\eta_{F}
is trivial. It can be easily verified that there exist an exact sequence
\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}_{2}\circ\mathbb{E}_{1}
of the form given in (
2.12.1
) and
a morphism
\mathbb{E}\rightarrow{\rm t}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime})
with morphisms
\mbox{id}_{{\rm t}(D)}
and
\eta_{F}
on the most left and right ending objects, respectively, such that
\mathbb{E}_{2}
is a trivial short exact sequence. Notice that

{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})={\rm q}(\mathbb{E}_{2})\circ{\rm q}(\mathbb{E}_{1})
is exact, and
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E}_{2})
is a trivial short exact sequence. we consider the composition of morphism of sequences

{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow{\rm qt}(\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime})\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime},

where the last morphism is
the canonical counit morphism of the adjunction. The composite has the morphisms
\xi_{D}
and
\mbox{id}_{{\rm q}(F)}
on the most left and right objects, respectively. So it can be factorized as

{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow\xi_{D}{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}.

\xi_{D}{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a trivial exact sequence which is equivalent to
\mathbb{E}^{\prime\prime}
. As a consequence,
\Theta_{F}^{2}
is an isomorphism for every
F
in
\mathcal{F}
. By mathematical induction,
\Theta_{F}^{n}
is an isomorphism for every
n\geq 1
.

∎

2.13. Example.

We assume that
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})
is equipped with the exact structure
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{E})
(see Example
2.4
). If
\mathcal{C}
has (co)kernels, then for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is an adjoint triple
(\operatorname{c}_{i},\operatorname{s}^{i},\operatorname{k}_{i})

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 14.65277pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.65277pt\raise 46.46399pt\raise-6.17506pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.17508pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathrm{s}^{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.65277pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
\mathrm{s}^{i}(C)
denotes the
i
th sphere chain complex of
C
, and
\mathrm{c}_{i}(X)
and
\mathrm{k}_{i}(X)
are the cokernel and kernel of differentials
X_{i+1}\longrightarrow X_{i}
and
X_{i}\longrightarrow X_{i-1}
, respectively. Note that for every
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the stalk functor
\mathrm{s}^{i}
is exact. So for any chain complex
X
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})
and
n\geq 1
, there are natural transformations

\Theta_{X}^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm c}_{i}(X),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(X,{\rm s}_{i}(-))

\Omega_{X}^{n}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n}(-,{\rm k}_{i}(X))\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm s}_{i}(-),X)

In general, the functors
\mathrm{c}_{i}
and
\mathrm{k}_{i}
are not exact (see Example
3.6
), and therefore,
\Theta_{X}^{n}
and
\Omega_{X}^{n}
may not be an isomorphism for an arbitrary
X
. Recall that a chain complex
X
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})
is said to be
acyclic
if every differential factors as
X_{n}\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X)\hookrightarrow X_{i-1}
in such a way that each sequence

\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X)}

is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
; see
[
Buh10
, Definition 10.1]
. We let
Acyc
denote the class of acyclic chain complexes in
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})
. By
[
Sto14
, Proposition 7.5]
, we already know that
Acyc
satisfies all conditions and their dual forms given in (
1.6
). As it will be shown in Example
3.6
, the functors
{\rm c}_{i}
and
{\rm k}_{i}
restricted on
Acyc
are exact, and by Lemma
2.12
, the natural transformations
\Theta_{X}^{n}
and
\Omega_{X}^{n}
are isomorphisms for every acyclic chain complex
X
.

2.14. Lemma.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be classes of objects in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively. For any integer
n\geq 1
,
C\in\mathcal{C}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}
, the followings hold:

(i)

Suppose that
{\rm t}
is exact,
\mathcal{D}
is an abelian category and
\mathcal{F}
and
{\rm q}
satisfy conditions given in (
1.6
). If for every
1\leq k\leq n
,

{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
, then the natural transformation

\Theta_{C}^{n+1}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}({\rm q}(C),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}(C,{\rm t}(-))

is a monomorphism.

(ii)

Suppose that
{\rm q}
is exact,
\mathcal{C}
is an abelian category and
\mathcal{G}
and
{\rm t}
satisfies dual of conditions given in (
1.6
). If for every
1\leq k\leq n
,

{\rm R^{k}_{\mathcal{G}}t}(D)=0
, then the natural transformation

\Omega_{D}^{n+1}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}(-,{\rm t}(D))\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}({\rm q}(-),D)

is a monomorphism.

Proof.

As pointed out in (
1.6
), using
\mathcal{F}
-resolutions, we have the
k
th derived functor
{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}:\ \mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{D}
satisfying
{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(F)=0
for every
F\in\mathcal{F}
and
k\geq 1
.

Consider a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
of the form

(2.14.1)

\mathbb{E}:\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.60416pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.60416pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.60417pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.60416pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.60416pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.42361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.42361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

with
F
in
\mathcal{F}
. It induces the following long exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}

(2.14.2)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 195.46677pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Suppose that
{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
for every
1\leq k\leq n
. Then the exact sequence (
2.14.2
) turns out to be of the form

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise q}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 183.557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

For a given object
D
in
\mathcal{D}
, applying the contravariant functor
\operatorname{Hom}(-,D)
, and subsequently, using the adjoint pair
({\rm q},{\rm t})
and Lemma
2.12
, we have the following commutative diagram of exact sequences

\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(F),D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{n}_{FD}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(K),D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{n}_{KD}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}({\rm q}(C),D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{n+1}_{CD}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}({\rm q}(F),D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{n+1}_{FD}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(F,{\rm t}(D))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(F,{\rm t}(D))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}(C,{\rm t}(D))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}(F,{\rm t}(D))}

Now, we apply mathematical induction on
n
. If
n=1
, then by Lemma
2.1
,
\Theta^{1}_{KD}
is a monomorphism, and therefore, so is
\Theta^{2}_{CD}
.

Now, suppose that the statement is true for some
n\geq 1
, and
{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
for every
1\leq k\leq n+1
. Then
{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(K)=0
for every
1\leq k\leq n
. By induction hypothesis,
\Theta^{n+1}_{KD}
is a monomorphism, then so is
\Theta^{n+2}_{CD}

∎

2.15. Proposition.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be classes of objects in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively. For any integer
n\geq 1
,
C\in\mathcal{C}
and
D\in\mathcal{D}
, the followings hold:

(i)

If
{\rm t}
is exact,
\mathcal{D}
is an abelian category, and
\mathcal{F}
and
{\rm q}
satisfy conditions given in (
1.6
), then the following are equivalent:

(a)

For every
1\leq k\leq n
,
{\rm L^{\mathcal{F}}_{k}q}(C)=0
;

(b)

For every
1\leq k\leq n
, the natural transformation

\Theta_{C}^{k}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{k}({\rm q}(C),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{k}(C,{\rm t}(-)),

is an isomorphism.

(ii)

If
{\rm q}
is exact,
\mathcal{C}
is an abelian category, and
\mathcal{G}
and
{\rm t}
satisfy dual conditions given in (
1.6
), then the followings hold:

(a)

For every
1\leq k\leq n
,
{\rm R^{k}_{\mathcal{G}}t}(D)=0

(b)

For every
1\leq k\leq n
, the natural transformation

\Omega_{D}^{k}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{k}(-,{\rm t}(D))\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{k}({\rm q}(-),D),

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
of the form

(2.15.1)

with
F
in
\mathcal{F}
. It induces the following long exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}

(2.15.2)

0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

We apply mathematical induction on
n
to prove the equivalence of the statements (a) and (b). If
{\rm L_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
, then the exact sequence (
2.15.2
) is turned out being of the form

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm q}(F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 132.63895pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 132.63895pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm q}(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 183.557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

which by Lemma
2.12
, yields
the following commutative diagram of exact sequences of functors

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}({\rm q}(F),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}({\rm q}(K),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(C),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{1}_{C}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(F),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{1}_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(K),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{1}_{K}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(F,{\rm t}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(F,{\rm t}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces]}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(C,{\rm t}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(F,{\rm t}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(K,{\rm t}(-))}

Using the fact that
\Theta^{1}_{CD}
and
\Theta^{1}_{KD}
are monomorphisms, one can easily show that
\Theta^{1}_{CD}
is in fact an isomorphism.

For the converse, suppose that the natural transformation

{\Theta^{1}_{C}:\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}(C),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(C,{\rm t}(-))}

is an isomorphism. By Proposition
2.10
,
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
, and hence,
{\rm L_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
.

Now, suppose that the equivalence (a)
\Leftrightarrow
(b) is true for any
n\geq 1
and any object in
\mathcal{C}
. Assume that
{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
for every
1\leq k\leq n+1
. By induction step, the natural transformation

\Theta^{k}_{C}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{k}({\rm q}(C),-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{k}(C,{\rm t}(-))

is an isomorphism for every
1\leq k\leq n
, and by Lemma
2.14
,
\Theta^{n+1}_{C}
is a monomorphism. And from the long exact sequence of derived functors
{\rm L^{\mathcal{F}}_{k}q}(-)
obtained through the short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}
, we have

0={\rm L_{k+1}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)\cong{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(K)

for every
1\leq k\leq n
. By induction step,
\Theta_{K}^{k}
is an isomorphism for every
1\leq k\leq n
, and by Lemma
2.14
,
\Theta_{K}^{n+1}
is a monomorphism.

As
{\rm L_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
,
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
, which induces the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}({\rm q}(K),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{n}_{K}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}({\rm q}(C),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{n+1}_{C}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}({\rm q}(F),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{n+1}_{F}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}(K,{\rm t}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Theta^{n+1}_{K}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n}(F,{\rm t}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces]}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}(C,{\rm t}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}(F,{\rm t}(-))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{n+1}(K,{\rm t}(-))}

with exact rows. From the diagram, it is easy to verify that
\Theta^{n+1}_{C}
is an epimorphism.

For the converse, suppose that the natural transformation
\Theta_{C}^{k}
is an isomorphism for every
1\leq k\leq n+1
. By induction hypothesis,
{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
for every
1\leq k\leq n
.
As a particular case,
{\rm L_{1}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(C)=0
, and therefore,
{\rm q}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{D}
. Applying five lemma, one can show that
\Theta_{K}^{k}
is an isomorphism for every
1\leq k\leq n
. Again, by induction hypothesis,
{\rm L_{k}^{\mathcal{F}}q}(K)=0
for every
1\leq k\leq n
. By shifting dimension, we have

{\rm L_{n+1}^{\mathcal{F}}}{\rm q}(C)\cong{L_{n}^{\mathcal{F}}}{\rm q}(K)=0
.

∎

2.16. Example.

Let
f:\ R\rightarrow S
a ring homomorphism. Consider the adjoint pair

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 18.50177pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 102.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.33699pt\raise-14.50696pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 18.50806pt\raise-5.19933pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
\mbox{res}:\ S\mbox{-Mod}\longrightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
denotes the functor of restriction of scalars through the ring homomorphism
f
. Note that
\operatorname{res}
is an exact functor, and

{{\rm L_{k}}(S\otimes_{R}-)=\mbox{Tor}_{R}^{k}(S,-)}.

By Proposition
2.15
-(i), for a left
R
-module
M
,
\operatorname{Tor}_{R}^{k}(S,M)=0
for every
1\leq k\leq n
if and only if
\Theta_{M}^{k}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{k}(S\otimes_{R}M,-)\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{k}(M,\operatorname{res}(-)),

is an isomorphism for every
1\leq k\leq n
.

Similarly, for the adjoint pair

\textstyle{S\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}}
\textstyle{R\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(S,-)}

By Proposition
2.15
, for a left
R
-module
N
,
\operatorname{Ext}^{k}(S,N)=0
for every
1\leq k\leq n
if and only if
\Omega_{N}^{k}:\ \operatorname{Ext}^{k}(-,\operatorname{Hom}(S,N))\Longrightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{k}(\operatorname{res}(-),N)
is an isomorphism for every
1\leq k\leq n
.

3.
Cotorsion pairs induced from adjoint pairs

In this section, we apply the results from Section 2 to investigate cotorsion pairs arising through an adjoint pair. We also provide characterizations of objects in the right/left perpendicular classes that cover well-known examples.

We keep assuming that
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
are exact categories. For a given family
\{x_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of functors from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
, and classes
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
of objects in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively, we let

\displaystyle x_{*}(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle:=\{x_{i}(F)\mid\ F\in\mathcal{F}\textrm{ and }i\in I\}

\displaystyle x_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle:=\{C\in\mathcal{C}\mid\ x_{i}(C)\in\mathcal{G},\textrm{ for every }i\in I\}.

An object in
\mathcal{C}
will be said to be
x_{*}
-(co)flat if it is
x_{i}
-(co)flat for every
i\in I
.

3.1. Theorem.

Let
\{({\rm q}_{i},{\rm t}_{i})\}_{i\in I}
be a family of adjoint pairs

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.03067pt\raise 15.18752pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm q}_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 90.6157pt\raise 2.60219pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 60.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 90.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.56886pt\raise-15.65279pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.65279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm t}_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 6.61276pt\raise-2.53061pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

For given cotorsion pairs
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
and
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively, the following statements hold.

(i)

Suppose that for every
i\in I
, every object in
\mathcal{F}
is
{\rm q}_{i}
-flat.

(a)

If for every
i\in I
,
{\rm t}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}_{i}(\mathcal{F}),-)
, then

({\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\perp}={\rm t}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G}).

(b)

If
\mathcal{F}
is a generating class in
\mathcal{C}
, then

({\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\perp}=\{D\in{\rm t}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G})\mid\ D\textrm{ is a }{\rm t}_{*}\textrm{-coflat object}\}.

(ii)

Suppose that for every
i\in I
, every objeect in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is
{\rm t}_{i}
-coflat.

(a)

If for every
i\in I
,
{\rm q}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,{\rm t}_{i}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))
, then

{}^{\perp}({\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))={\rm q}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}).

(b)

If
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is a cogenerating class in
\mathcal{D}
, then

{}^{\perp}({\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))=\{C\in{\rm q}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\mid\ C\textrm{ is a }{\rm q}_{*}\textrm{-flat object}\}.

Proof.

We only prove the first statement, as the other can be proved by dual arguments. Firstly, as
\Omega^{1}_{F}
is a monomorphism for every
F\in\mathcal{F}
by Lemma
2.1
-(ii),

({\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\perp}\subseteq{\rm t}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G}).

If for every
i\in I
,
{\rm t}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}_{i}(\mathcal{F}),-)
, then by Lemma
2.1
-(iii),

({\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\perp}={\rm t}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G}).

On the other hand, if
\mathcal{F}
is a generating class, then by Lemma
2.9
-(i), any object in
({\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F}))^{\perp}
is a
{\rm t}_{i}
-coflat object for every
i\in I
. The converse inclusion
\supseteq
in (i)-(b) is immediate by Lemma
2.1
-(i).

∎

3.2. Remark.

Note that if for every
i\in I
,
{\rm q}_{i}
is exact,
\mathcal{C}
is an abelian category, and the
k
th right derived functor
{\rm R_{\mathcal{G}}^{k}t_{i}}
exists as described in (
1.6
), then by Proposition
2.10
-(ii) and Proposition
2.15
-(ii), for every
i\in I
, an object
D
in
\mathcal{D}
is
{\rm t}_{i}
-coflat is equivalent to
{\rm R^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}t}_{i}(D)=0
. In particular, if
\mathcal{D}
is an abelian category with enough injectives, and
\mathcal{C}
has enough projectives then the classes
\Psi(\mathcal{G})
and
\Phi(\mathcal{F^{\prime}})
defined in
[
HJ19
, Definition 1.5]
are exactly the classes
{\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp}
and
{}^{\perp}{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{G^{\prime}})
described in Theorem
3.1
-(i)-(b) and (ii)-(b), respectively.

3.3. Corollary.

Let
\{({\rm q}_{i},{\rm t}_{i},{\rm p_{i}})\}_{i\in I}
be a family of adjoint triples

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.03067pt\raise 25.18753pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm q}_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 90.6157pt\raise 5.20435pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.24998pt\raise-25.18752pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm p_{i}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 90.6157pt\raise-5.20435pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 30.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 60.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 90.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.56886pt\raise-5.65277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.65279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm t}_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.61111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

For given cotorsion pairs
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
and
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, respectively, the following hold:

(i)

Suppose that for every
i\in I
,
{\rm q}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{F},{\rm t}_{i}(-))
.

(a)

If for every
i\in I
,
{\rm t}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}_{i}(\mathcal{F}),-)
, then

{\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp}={\rm t}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G});

(b)

If
\mathcal{F}
is a generating class in
\mathcal{C}
, then

{\rm q}_{*}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp}=\{D\in{\rm t}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G})\mid\ D\textrm{ is a }{\rm t}_{*}\textrm{-coflat object}\}.

(ii)

Suppose that for every
i\in I
,
{\rm p_{i}}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm t}_{i}(-),\mathcal{G})
.

(a)

If for every
i\in I
,
{\rm t}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,{\rm p_{i}}(\mathcal{G}))
, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm p}_{*}(\mathcal{G})={\rm t}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{F});

(b)

If
\mathcal{G}
is a cogenerating class in
\mathcal{C}
, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm p}_{*}(\mathcal{G})=\{D\in{\rm t}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{F})\mid\ D\textrm{ is a }{\rm t}_{*}\textrm{-flat object}\}.

(iii)

Suppose that for every
i\in I
,
{\rm t}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},{\rm p}_{i}(-))
.

(a)

If for every
i\in I
,
{\rm p}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm t}_{i}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}),-)
, then

{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{\perp}={\rm p}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}).

(b)

If
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
is a generating class in
\mathcal{D}
, then

{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{\perp}=\{C\in{\rm p}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})\mid\ C\textrm{ is a }{\rm p}_{*}\textrm{-coflat object}\}.

(iv)

Suppose that for every
i\in I
,
{\rm t}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm q}_{i}(-),\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.

(a)

If for every
i\in I
,
{\rm q}_{i}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,{\rm t}_{i}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime}))
, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})={\rm q}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}).

(b)

If
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
is a cogenerating class in
\mathcal{D}
, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm t}_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})=\{C\in{\rm q}_{*}^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\mid\ C\textrm{ is a }{\rm q}_{*}\textrm{-flat object}\}.

Proof.

Apply Theorem
3.1
.
∎

3.4. Remark.

Since for every
i\in I
, the middle functor
{\rm t}_{i}
given in Corollary
3.3
is both right and left adjoint functor, it preserves all (co)limits, but it is not necessarily an exact functor between exact categories. However, it is exact whenever
\mathcal{C}
is an abelian category.

3.5. Example.

Consider Example
2.4
. For a given cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
in
\mathcal{C}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we let

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{F})_{i}

\displaystyle:=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})\mid\ X_{i}\in\mathcal{F}\};

\displaystyle\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{G})_{i}

\displaystyle:=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})\mid\ X_{i}\in\mathcal{G}\}.

As
\mbox{D}^{i}
and
\operatorname{ev}_{i}
are exact functors, by Corollary
3.3
, there are two cotorsion pairs in
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})

({}^{\perp}\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{G})_{i},\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{G})_{i})\quad\textrm{ and }\quad(\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{F})_{i},\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{F})_{i}^{\perp})

generated by
\mbox{D}^{i}(\mathcal{F})
and cogenerated by
\mbox{D}^{i+1}(\mathcal{G})
, respectively. If we consider all
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have the following cotorsion pairs

({}^{\perp}\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{G}),\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{G}))\quad\textrm{ and }\quad(\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{F}),\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp});

see
[
Gil08
, Proposition 3.2]
.

If
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
is a cotorsion pair in
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})
, then

\displaystyle\mbox{ev}_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\{C\in\mathcal{C}\mid\ \mbox{D}^{i}(C)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\textrm{ for every }i\in\mathbb{Z}\};

{}^{\perp}\mbox{ev}_{*}(\mathcal{G}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\{C\in\mathcal{C}\mid\ \mbox{D}^{i}(C)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\textrm{ for every }i\in\mathbb{Z}\};

see
[
Gil08
, Lemma 3.5]
.

3.6. Example.

Consider Example
2.13
. Let
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
be a cotorsion pair in
\mathcal{C}
. Note that
\mathrm{k}_{i}
doesn’t preserves all short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathrm{s}^{i}(\mathcal{F}),-)
for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
; for example, consider the canonical short exact sequence

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.77579pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.77579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{s}^{i-1}(F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.77579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.77579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.77579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{D}^{i}(F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.34122pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 98.34122pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{s}^{i}(F)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
F
is a non-zero object in
\mathcal{F}
. On the other hand, if
\mathcal{F}
is a generating class in
\mathcal{C}
, then

\mathrm{s}^{i}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp}=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})\mid\ \mathrm{k}_{i}(X)\in\mathcal{G}\textrm{ and }X\textrm{ is a }\mathrm{k}_{i}\textrm{-coflat object}\}.

We now show that a chain complex
X
in
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})
is a
\mathrm{k}_{i}
-coflat object if and only if the canonical sequence

\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X)}

is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
.

Suppose that
X
is a
\mathrm{k}_{i}
-coflat object for some
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, then consider the canonical morphism
f_{i-1}:\mathrm{s}^{i-1}(\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X))\longrightarrow X
, which induces the degreewise split short exact sequence in
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})

\mathbb{E}:\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{cone}(f_{i-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 104.73912pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 104.73912pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{s}^{i}(\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
\operatorname{cone}(f_{i-1})
is the cone of the morphism
f_{i}
. By assumption,
\mathrm{k}_{i}(\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
, and it is of the form

\mathrm{k}_{i}(\mathbb{E}):\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.72716pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.12587pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 82.12587pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Conversely, suppose that the canonical sequence

\mathrm{k}_{i}(\mathbb{E}):\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.72716pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.12587pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 82.12587pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}
.
By Lemma
2.8
, it is enough to show that
\mathrm{k}_{i}
preserves all short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathrm{s}^{i}(-),X)
. Consider a short exact sequence in
\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})
of the form

\mathbb{E}:\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{s}^{i}(C)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Without lost of generality, we can assume that
X_{j}=Y_{j}
for every
i\neq j\in\mathbb{Z}
.
The short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}
induces the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{X_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{i-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{i-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{i-2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

By assumption,
\mathcal{C}
has kernels, and therefore, the morphism
Y_{i}\longrightarrow\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(Y)
is in fact an admissible epic.
Applying Snake Lemma
[
Buh10
, Corollary 8.13]
to the diagram

\textstyle{X_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i-1}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

we obtain the short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}

\mathrm{k}_{i}(\mathbb{E}):\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.72716pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.72716pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrm{k}_{i}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.13982pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 94.13982pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C=\mathrm{k}_{i}(\mathrm{s}^{i}(C))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

If we consider all integers
i
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{s}^{*}(\mathcal{F})^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})\mid\ \mathrm{k}_{*}(X)\in\mathcal{G}\textrm{ and }X\textrm{ is a }\mathrm{k}_{*}\textrm{-coflat object}\}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})\mid\ \mathrm{k}_{*}(X)\in\mathcal{G}\textrm{ and }X\textrm{ is acyclic}\}

Similarly,
\mathrm{c}_{i}
doesn’t preserves all short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,\mathrm{s}^{i}(\mathcal{G}))

for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, and if
\mathcal{G}
is a cogenerating class in
\mathcal{C}
, then

{}^{\perp}\mathrm{s}^{*}(\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})\mid\ \mathrm{c}_{*}(X)\in\mathcal{F}\textrm{ and }X\textrm{ is a }\mathrm{c}_{*}\textrm{-flat object}\}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(\mathcal{C})\mid\ \mathrm{c}_{*}(X)\in\mathcal{F}\textrm{ and }X\textrm{ is acyclic}\}.

3.7. Example.

Consider the following adjoint triple

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.84207pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.84207pt\raise Since
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
is an injective abelian group,
\operatorname{Hom}(-,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
is an exact functor. If
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
is a cotorsion pair in
R\mbox{-Mod}
, then

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{G},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{\perp}=\{M\in\mbox{Mod-}R\mid\ \operatorname{Hom}(M,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\in\mathcal{F}\}

{}^{\perp}\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{F},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})=\{M\in\mbox{Mod-}R\mid\ \operatorname{Hom}(M,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\in\mathcal{G}\}.

3.8. Example.

Consider the degreewise pure and split exact structures on
\mathbf{C}(R)
and
\mathbf{C}(R^{\textrm{op}})
, respectively. The adjoint triple of Example
3.7
can be extended to the following adjoint triple of functoors

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 19.32814pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.32814pt\raise 37.00859pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(-,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.32814pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

which are exact.
We let
\mbox{Acyc}(R^{\textrm{op}})
and
\mbox{K-Inj}(R^{\textrm{op}})
denote the classes of acyclic and k-injective chain complexes of
R^{\textrm{op}}
-modules: see
[
Spa88
]
. It can be easily verifed that
(\mbox{Acyc}(R^{\textrm{op}}),\mbox{K-Inj}(R^{\textrm{op}}))
is a cotorsion pair in
\mathbf{C}(R^{\textrm{op}})
. So we have

\displaystyle{}^{\perp}\operatorname{Hom}(\mbox{Acyc}(R^{\textrm{op}}),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(R)\mid\ \operatorname{Hom}(X,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\in\mbox{K-Inj}(R^{\textrm{op}})\}

\displaystyle=\{X\in\mathbf{C}(R)\mid\ X\textrm{ is k-flat}\}

The last equality follows from the isomorphism

\operatorname{H}_{i}(\operatorname{Hom}(Y\otimes_{R}X,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbf{K}(R^{\textrm{op}})}(Y,\operatorname{Hom}(X,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})),

where
Y\in\mathbf{C}(R^{\textrm{op}})
and
Y\otimes_{R}X
denotes the total tensor product of chain complexes.

3.9. Example.

Let
\{f_{i}:R\rightarrow S_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a family of ring homomorphisms. For every
i\in I
, consider the adjoint triple

2.16
,
where
\mbox{res}_{i}:\ S_{i}\mbox{-Mod}\longrightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
denotes the functor of restriction of scalars through
f_{i}
. They induce a family of adjoint triples

0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 42.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 102.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{i\in I}S_{i}\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.17741pt\raise 5.12848pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.12848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}_{i}\circ\pi_{i}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Note that
\operatorname{res}_{i}\circ\pi_{i}
is an exact functor. Let
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
be a cotorsion pair in
R\mbox{-Mod}
.

(i)

By Lemma
2.15
, for every
i\in I
,

(S_{i}\otimes_{R}-)
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\mathcal{F},\operatorname{res}_{i}(-))
if and only if
\mbox{Tor}_{R}^{1}(S_{i},F)=0
for every
F\in\mathcal{F}
. In such case,

(\iota_{*}\circ(S_{*}\otimes_{R}\mathcal{F}))^{\perp}=\{(N_{i})_{i\in I}\in\prod_{i\in I}S_{i}\mbox{-Mod}\mid\operatorname{res}_{i}(N_{i})\in\mathcal{G}\textrm{ for every }i\in I\}.

(ii)

By Lemma
2.15
, for every
i\in I
the functor
\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(S_{i},-)
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(\operatorname{res}_{i}(-),\mathcal{G})
if and only if
\operatorname{Ext}_{R}^{1}(S_{i},G)=0
for every
G\in\mathcal{G}
, that is,
\operatorname{res}_{i}(S_{i})\in\mathcal{F}
. In such case,

{}^{\perp}(\iota_{*}\circ\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(S_{*},\mathcal{G}))=\{(N_{i})_{i\in I}\in\prod_{i\in I}S_{i}\mbox{-Mod}\mid\operatorname{res}_{i}(N_{i})\in\mathcal{F}\textrm{ for every }i\in I\}.

On the other hand, by Lemma
1.8
, any cotorsion pair in
\prod_{i\in I}S_{i}\mbox{-Mod}
is just a family
\{(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G}_{i})\}_{i\in I}
of cotorsion pairs
(\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathcal{G}_{i})
in
S_{i}\mbox{-Mod}
.

(i)

As for every
i\in I
,
\mathcal{F}_{i}
is a generating class in
S_{i}\mbox{-Mod}
,

\mbox{res}_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{*})^{\perp}=\{N\in R\mbox{-Mod}\mid\ \operatorname{Hom}_{R}(S_{i},N)\in\mathcal{G}_{i},\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R}(S_{i},N)=0\textrm{ for every }i\in I\}

(ii)

As for every
i\in I
,
\mathcal{G}_{i}
is a cogenerating class in
S_{i}\mbox{-Mod}
,

{}^{\perp}\mbox{res}_{*}(\mathcal{G}_{*})=\{N\in R\mbox{-Mod}\mid\ S_{i}\otimes_{R}N\in\mathcal{F}_{i},\mbox{Tor}_{R}^{1}(S_{i},N)=0\textrm{ for every }i\in I\}

3.10. Example.

We know consider a very particular case of Example
3.9
. Let
R
be a commutative ring. For a given element
r
of
R
, consider the localization of
R
at
r
, denoted by
R[r^{-1}]
, together with the ring homomorphism
R\rightarrow R[r^{-1}]
. As
R[r^{-1}]
is a flat
R
-
R
-bimodule, the functor
R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}-
is exact, and therefore,
\operatorname{Tor}_{R}^{1}(R[r^{-1}],-)=0
. Note that for any
M\in R\mbox{-Mod}
,

R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}N\cong N[r^{-1}].

For a given cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{F}_{r},\mathcal{G}_{r})
in
R[r^{-1}]\mbox{-Mod}
, we have

\begin{split}{}^{\perp}(\operatorname{res}(\mathcal{G}_{r}))&=\{N\in R\mbox{-Mod}\mid N[r^{-1}]\in\mathcal{F}_{r}\};\\
(\operatorname{res}(\mathcal{F}_{r}))^{\perp}&=\{N\in R\mbox{-Mod}\mid\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(R[r^{-1}],N)\in\mathcal{G}_{r}\textrm{ and }\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R}(R[r^{-1}],N)=0\}.\end{split}

As a particular case, consider the trivial cotorsion pair
(\mbox{Proj}_{r},R[r^{-1}]\mbox{-Mod})
in
R[r^{-1}]\mbox{-Mod}
, where
\mbox{Proj}_{r}
denotes the class of projective
R[r^{-1}]
-modules. So we have

\begin{split}{}^{\perp}(\operatorname{res}(R[r^{-1}]\mbox{-Mod}))&=\{N\in R\mbox{-Mod}\mid N[r^{-1}]\in\mbox{Proj}\}\\
(\operatorname{res}(\mbox{Proj}_{r}))^{\perp}&=\{N\in R\mbox{-Mod}\mid\mbox{Ext}_{R}^{1}(R[r^{-1}],N)=0\}\\
&=\{r\textrm{-contraadjusted $R$-modules}\}\end{split}

Consider the family of adjoint triples

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 18.50177pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.85382pt\raise 26.99504pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.24501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{r}\circ(R[r^{-1}]\otimes_{R}-)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 124.88461pt\raise 8.63991pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.61766pt\raise-26.99504pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.24501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{r}\circ\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(R[r^{-1}],-)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 104.9949pt\raise-15.49959pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 42.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 72.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 102.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{r\in R}R[r^{-1}]\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.87234pt\raise 5.12848pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.12848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\operatorname{res}_{r}\circ\pi_{r}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.50177pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

and the family
\{(\mbox{Proj}_{r},R[r^{-1}]\mbox{-Mod})\}_{r\in R}
of the trivial cotorsion pairs.

\begin{split}(\operatorname{res}_{*}(\mbox{Proj}_{*}))^{\perp}&=\{N\in R\mbox{-Mod}\mid\mbox{Ext}_{R}^{1}(R[r^{-1}],N)=0\textrm{ for every }r\in R\}\\
&=\{\textrm{contraadjusted $R$-modules}\}\end{split}

R
-modules in
{}^{\perp}((\operatorname{res}_{*}(\mbox{Proj}_{*}))^{\perp})
are called very flat
R
-modules; see
[
Pos17
, Section 1.1]

4.
Functor categories and Evaluation Functor

In this and upcoming sections, we center on functor categories in order to obtain further results on cotorsion pairs induced by adjoint triples.

Throughout,
R
denotes a ring with identity, and
\mathcal{A}
denotes a small preadditive category. For any object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, we let
R_{A}
denote the endomorphism ring
\operatorname{Hom}(A,A)
. Note that for any two objects
A
and
B
in
\mathcal{A}
,
\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)
has a canonical
R_{B}
-
R_{A}
-bimodule structure induced from the composition rule

(4.0.1)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(B,B)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)}

Since for every morphisms
g:A\rightarrow B
,
f:B\rightarrow B
and
h:B\rightarrow C
in
\mathcal{A}
,
{h\circ(f\circ g)=h\circ f\circ g=(h\circ f)\circ g}
, the composition
is canonically factorized over
\operatorname{Hom}(B,C)\otimes_{R_{B}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(B,C)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(A,C)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(B,C)\otimes_{R_{B}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

4.1. Evaluation Functor.

We let
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
denote the category of additive functors from
\mathcal{A}
to
R\mbox{-Mod}
. The evaluation functor

\operatorname{ev}:\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\otimes\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}

is the functor defined by
\operatorname{ev}(X,A)=X(A)=:\operatorname{ev}_{A}(X)
.

For a given additive functor
X:\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
and objects
A
and
B
in
\mathcal{A}
,
X(A)
has a canonical left
R_{A}
-module structure defined by
f\cdot a:=X(f)(a)
, where
f\in R_{A}
and
a\in X(A)
. So it is easy to verify that the associated homomorphism of abelian groups

X_{AB}:\ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.32121pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.32121pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.32121pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 51.32121pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(X(A),X(B))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

induces the following left
R_{B}
and
R_{A}
-module homomorphisms, respectively,

\overline{X}_{AB}:\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\otimes_{R_{A}}X(A)\longrightarrow X(B),\quad g\otimes a\rightsquigarrow X(f)(x),

\underline{X}_{AB}:X(A)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),X(B)),\quad\underline{X}_{AB}(a)(g)=X(g)(a).

Besides, as for every
f\in R_{A}
,
X(f)
is an
R
-module homomorphism, we have

f\cdot(r\cdot a)=r\cdot(f\cdot a)

for every
a\in X(A)
and
r\in R
.

If
\alpha=\{\alpha_{A}\}_{A\in\mathcal{A}}:X\Rightarrow Y
is a natural transformation of additive functors
{X,Y:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}}
, then for any
f\in R_{A}
and
a\in X(A)
, we have

\alpha_{A}(f\cdot a)=\alpha_{A}(X(f)(a))=Y(f)(\alpha_{A}(a))=f\cdot\alpha_{A}(a).

In other words, for every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
,
\alpha_{A}:X(A)\rightarrow Y(A)
is an
R_{A}
-module homomorphism. So
the evaluation functor
\operatorname{ev}_{A}:\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
has also image in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}

(4.1.1)

\textstyle{\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\otimes\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ev}_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ev}_{A}}
\textstyle{R\mbox{-Mod}}
\textstyle{R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}}

4.2. Remark.

Similarly,
the evaluation functor
\operatorname{ev}_{A}:\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A}^{\textrm{op}},R\mbox{-Mod})\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
has image in
R_{A}^{\textrm{op}}\mbox{-Mod}=\mbox{Mod-}R_{A}
.

From now on, we assume that
\mathcal{A}
satisfies the following condition.

4.3. Hypothesis.

For every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, there exists a ring morphism
\delta_{A}:R\rightarrow R_{A}
such that for every morphism
f:A\rightarrow B
in
\mathcal{A}
, the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{R_{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{B}}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{A}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)}
\textstyle{R_{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}}

Hypothesis
4.3
implies that for every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
,

\operatorname{Hom}(A,-)\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\operatorname{Hom}(-,A)\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A}^{op},\mbox{Mod-}R).

Indeed, the
R_{B}
-
R_{A}
-bimodule
\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)
is clearly
R
-
R
-bimodule by restriction of scalars. Besides, it satisfies the following equality

(4.3.1)

r\cdot f=\delta_{B}(r)\circ f=f\circ\delta_{A}(r)=f\cdot r,

for every
r\in R
and
f\in\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)
.
Besides, for any morphisms
f:A\rightarrow B
and
g:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}
and an element
r
in
R
, we have

g_{*}(r\cdot f)=g_{*}(f\circ\delta_{A}(r))=g\circ f\circ\delta_{A}(r)=\delta_{B^{\prime}}(r)\circ(g\circ f)=r\cdot(g_{*}(f)).

In other words,
g_{*}:\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(A,B^{\prime})
is a left
R
-module homomorphism. Similarly,
g^{*}:\operatorname{Hom}(B^{\prime},A)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)
is a right
R
-module homomorphism.

Furthermore, the evaluation functor
\operatorname{ev}_{A}
given in (
4.1.1
) is factorized over the forgetful functor
U:R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
, and the composition rule
is canonically factorized as

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(B,C)\otimes_{R}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(A,C)}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(B,C)\otimes_{R_{B}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

4.4. Remark.

The conditions imposed on
\mathcal{A}
in Hypothesis (
4.3
) is equivalent to being
\mathcal{A}
an
R
-
R
-bimodule enriched category.

4.5. Example.

We let
\mathcal{A}
be the category whose set of objects is
\mathbb{Z}
, and

\operatorname{Hom}(i,j):=\begin{cases}R,&i=j\textrm{ or }i=j+1\\
0,&\textrm{ otherwise}\end{cases}

It is easy to verify that
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
is equivalent to the category
\mathbf{C}(R)
of chain complexes of left
R
-modules.

4.6. Example.

Consider a Morita context ring
\begin{bmatrix}T&M\\
N&S\end{bmatrix}
, that is,
T
,
S
are rings,
M
and
N
are
T
-
S
and
S
-
T
-bimodules, respectively, together with bimodule homomorphisms

M\otimes_{S}N\rightarrow T
, and
N\otimes_{T}M\rightarrow S
.

We let
\mathcal{A}
be the category with two objects
A
and
B
and with morphisms
R_{A}:=T
,
R_{B}:=S
,
\operatorname{Hom}(A,B):=N
,
\operatorname{Hom}(B,A):=M
together with the canonical composition rule induced from the ring multiplications and bimodule homomorphisms of the given Morita context ring.
It is immediate that

\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},\operatorname{Ab})\cong\begin{bmatrix}T&M\\
N&S\end{bmatrix}\mbox{-Mod}.

4.7.

For any object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
and a left
R_{A}
-module
M
, the authors in
[
CT13
, Section 2]
define the following
\operatorname{Ab}
-valued functors

\displaystyle{\rm q}_{A}(M)(-)

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{Hom}(A,-)\otimes_{R_{A}}M

\displaystyle{\rm p}_{A}(M)(-)

\displaystyle:=\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\operatorname{Hom}(-,A),M),

By the hypothesis imposed on
\mathcal{A}
in (
4.3
), they are indeed
R\mbox{-Mod}
-valued functors, and hence, induce the functors

{\rm q}_{A},{\rm p}_{A}:R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\longrightarrow\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod}).

4.8. Proposition.

For every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
,
({\rm q}_{A},\operatorname{ev}_{A},{\rm p}_{A})
is an adjoint triple.

Proof.

By
[
CT13
, Proposition 1]
, viewing any functor
X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
as an
\operatorname{Ab}
-valued functor, we already know that the natural transformation

\operatorname{Nat}({\rm q}_{A}(M),X)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(M,X(A)),\quad\alpha\rightsquigarrow M\cong R_{A}\otimes_{R_{A}}M\xrightarrow{\alpha_{A}}X(A)

is injective. Surjectivity follows from the fact that the induced morphism

\overline{X}_{AB}:\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\otimes_{R_{A}}X(A)\rightarrow X(B)

is a left
R
-module homomorphism.

By dual arguments, it is easy to verify that
(\operatorname{ev}_{A},{\rm p}_{A})
is an adjoint pair, as well.

∎

We let
{\rm q},{\rm p}:\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\rightarrow\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
denote the functors
\oplus_{A\in A}{\rm q}_{A}
and
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm p}_{A}
, respectively.

4.9. Proposition.

({\rm q},(\operatorname{ev}_{A})_{A\in\mathcal{A}},{\rm p})
is an adjoint triple.

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 35.38377pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-35.38377pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.42543pt\raise 25.18753pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm q}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 139.35709pt\raise 7.99814pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern
