4.10. Hypothesis.

From now on,
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
is assumed to be equipped with a fixed exact structure
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}_{A}
, unless otherwise is stated. Accordingly, we assume that
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
is equipped with the exact structure
\mathcal{E}
, which consists of the short exact sequences
\mathbb{E}
such that
\operatorname{ev}_{A}(\mathbb{E})
belongs to
\mathcal{E}_{A}
for every
{A\in\mathcal{A}}
. Clearly,
\mathcal{E}
is a weakly idempotent complete exact structure on
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
.

4.11. Proposition.

For any object
A\in\mathcal{A}
, the following statements hold.

i)

\operatorname{ev}_{A}
is an exact functor for every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
.

ii)

If
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A}
is a generating class in

\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then so is
\{{\rm q}_{A}(\mathcal{F}_{A})\}_{A\in\mathcal{A}}
in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
.

iii)

If
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A}
is a cogenerating class in

\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then so is
\{{\rm p}_{A}(\mathcal{F}_{A})\}_{A\in\mathcal{A}}
in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
.

Proof.

The first statement is immediate. It also implies that for any
\mathcal{E}_{A}
-projective object
M
in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
,
{\rm q}_{A}(M)
is projective in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
, as well.

Now we prove the second statement. The dual arguments can be applied for the third statement. Let
{X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})}
. For any object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, we consider an admissible epic
{F_{A}\twoheadrightarrow X(A)}
in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
with
F_{A}\in\mathcal{F}_{A}
. By adjunction given in Proposition
4.9
, there exists a natural transformation
\alpha:\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}(F_{A})\Rightarrow X
. It is in fact an admissible epic in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
. Indeed, for every
{B\in\mathcal{A}}
, the morphism
\alpha_{B}
is the coproduct of the morphism

\oplus_{B\neq A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}(F_{A})(B)\longrightarrow X(B)
and the admissible epic

{\rm q}_{B}(F_{B})(B)\cong F_{B}\twoheadrightarrow X(B)
in
R_{B}\mbox{-Mod}
. By Proposition
1.3
,
\alpha_{B}
is an admissible epic in
R_{B}\mbox{-Mod};\mathcal{E}_{B}
.
∎

As a particular case of Proposition
4.11
, for any functor
X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
the counit morphism
{\rm q}((X(A))_{A\in\mathcal{A}})\Rightarrow X
and the unit morphism
X\Rightarrow{\rm p}((X(A))_{A\in\mathcal{A}})
are an admissible epic and monic, respectively.

Applying Corollary
3.3
for the adjoint triple given in Proposition
4.9
, we obtain a more general form of Example
3.5
.

4.12. Proposition.

Let
(\ \prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
be a cotorsion pair in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
.

i)

If for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
, any object in
\mathcal{F}_{A}
is
{\rm q}_{A}
-flat, then we have

{\rm q}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}=\{X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\mid\ X(A)\in\mathcal{G}_{A}\textrm{ for every }A\in\mathcal{A}\}.

ii)

If for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
, every object in
\mathcal{G}_{A}
is
{\rm p}_{A}
-coflat, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm p}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})=\{X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\mid\ X(A)\in\mathcal{F}_{A}\textrm{ for every }A\in\mathcal{A}\}

4.13. Example.

Consider the functor category representation of
\mathbf{C}(R)
as given in Example
4.5
. For a given
i\in\mathbb{Z}
, the functors
{\rm q}_{i}
and
{\rm p}_{i}
are precisely the disc chain complexes
\operatorname{D}^{i}
and
\operatorname{D}^{i+1}
functors (see Example
2.4
). Since
\operatorname{D}^{i}
is an exact functor, the cotorsion pairs in Example
3.5
are easily obtained by Proposition
4.12
.

As a particular case, if we consider the absolute exact structure on
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then we have the following result.

4.14. Corollary.

Let
(\ \prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
be a cotorsion pair in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
equipped with the absolute exact structure.

i)

If
{\rm L_{1}p}_{A}(\mathcal{F}_{A})=\mbox{Tor}_{R_{A}}^{1}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,-),\mathcal{F}_{A})=0
for every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, then we have

{\rm q}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}=\{X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\mid\ X(A)\in\mathcal{G}_{A}\textrm{ for every }A\in\mathcal{A}\}.

ii)

If
{\rm R^{1}q}_{A}(\mathcal{G}_{A})=\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{R_{A}}(\operatorname{Hom}(-,A),\mathcal{G}_{A})=0
for every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm p}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})=\{X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\mid\ X(A)\in\mathcal{F}_{A}\textrm{ for every }A\in\mathcal{A}\}.

4.15. Remark.

The categories
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
and
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
equipped with the absolute exact structures have enough injectives and projectives. Under the conditions given in Corollary
4.14
-(i), if
(\ \prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
is hereditary, then clearly the class
{\rm q}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}
is closed under quotients of monomorphism, and therefore the cotorsion pair generated by
{\rm q}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})
is hereditary, as well. By the same reason, under the conditions given in Corollary
4.14
-(ii), the cotorsion pair cogenerated by
\perp{\rm p}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
is hereditary, as well.

5.
Stalk functor in functor categories

We continue studying on
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
. Unless otherwise stated, we still assume that
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
is equipped with the exact structure
\mathcal{E}
induced from a fixed exact structure
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}_{A}
on
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
as stated in (
4.10
). In this section, we investigate cotorsion pairs arising through ‘stalk functor’. In order to do it, we need to impose further conditions on
\mathcal{A}
.

5.1. Hypothesis.

We assume that
\mathcal{A}
satisfies the following condition: For every two objects
A\neq B
in
\mathcal{A}
, the composition rule

\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\otimes_{R_{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(B,B)

is zero.

5.2. Example.

Consider a Morita context ring as given in Example
4.6
. Whenever the bimodule homomorphisms

M\otimes_{S}N\rightarrow T
and
N\otimes_{T}M\rightarrow S
are zero, it is called
Morita context ring with zero trace ideals
. In particular, when
M=0
or
N=0
, then it is called
a formal
triangular matrix ring
.

The associated category
\mathcal{A}
to a Morita context ring with zero trace ideals as presented in Example
4.6
satisfies Hypothesis
5.1
.

5.3. Stalk functor.

For every objects
A
and
B
in
\mathcal{A}
and
M\in R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, we define

{\rm s}_{A}(M)(B):=\begin{cases}0;&A\neq B\\
M;&A=B\end{cases}

For a given morphism
f:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}
, we define a morphism

{\rm s}_{A}(M)(f):{\rm s}_{A}(M)(B)\rightarrow{\rm s}_{A}(M)(B^{\prime})

as follows: If
B\neq A
or
B^{\prime}\neq A
, then
{\rm s}_{A}(M)(f):=0
; if
B=A=B^{\prime}
, then
{\rm s}_{A}(M)(f):=f\cdot(-)
, the left
R_{A}
-module scalar multiplication on
M
.

5.4. Proposition.

For every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
,
{\rm s}_{A}:R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\longrightarrow\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
is a functor.

Proof.

The only thing that we need to show is that for every left
R_{A}
-module
M
,
{\rm s}_{A}(M)(-)
belongs to
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
.

It is immediate that for every
B\in\mathcal{A}
,
{\rm s}_{A}(M)(B)
is a left
R
-module. By (
4.3.1
), for a given morphism
f\in R_{A}
, the morphism
{\rm s}_{A}(M)(f)
is an
R
-module homomorphism.

Clearly, for every
B\in B
,
{\rm s}_{A}(M)(\operatorname{id}_{B})=\operatorname{id}
. It is left to prove that
{\rm s}_{A}(M)(-)
preserves compositions. Consider two arbitrary morphisms
f:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
and
g:B^{\prime}\rightarrow B^{\prime\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}
. It is immediate that

{\rm s}_{A}(M)(g\circ f)={\rm s}_{A}(M)(g)\circ{\rm s}_{A}(M)(f)

whenever
B\neq A
or
B^{\prime\prime}\neq A
. Suppose that
B=A=B^{\prime\prime}
. If
B^{\prime}=A
, from the associativity of the scalar multiplication,

{\rm s}_{A}(M)(g\circ f):=(g\circ f)\cdot(-)=(g\cdot f)(-)=g\cdot(f\cdot-)={\rm s}_{A}(M)(g)\circ{\rm s}_{A}(M)(f).

If
B^{\prime}\neq A
, by Hypothesis
5.1
,
g\circ f=0
, and hence,

{\rm s}_{A}(M)(g\circ f)=0={\rm s}_{A}(M)(g)\circ{\rm s}_{A}(M)(f).

∎

5.5. Remark.

The stalk functor introduced in (
5.3
) differs subtly from the one defined in
[
HJ22
, Definition 7.9]
. For instance, let
\mathcal{A}
be the additive category generated by the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}

Then
\mathcal{A}
has only one object with the endomorphism ring
R_{A}=\mathbb{Z}[x]
. For every polynomial
p(x)\in\mathbb{Z}[x]
, we have
p(x)=p(0)+\overline{p}(x)\in\mathbb{Z}\oplus\langle x\rangle=\mathbb{Z}[x]
. We let
S_{A}:\operatorname{Ab}\rightarrow\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},\operatorname{Ab})
denote the functor defined in
[
HJ22
, Proposition 7.15]
.

\textstyle{\mathbb{Z}[x]\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm s}_{A}}
\textstyle{\mbox{Add}(\mathbb{Z},\operatorname{Ab})}
\textstyle{Ab\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{S_{A}}

If
M\in\mathbb{Z}[x]\mbox{-Mod}
, then
S_{A}(M)(p(x))=p(0)\cdot(-)\neq{\rm s}_{A}(M)(p(x))
.

In (
5.6
), we introduce several crucial constructions which will be used both for the left/right of
{\rm s}_{A}
, and to induce certain short exact sequences in Proposition
5.18
.

5.6.

Let
X:\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
be an additive functor, and
A
be an object in
\mathcal{A}
.
By the universal property of coproducts, the family

-module homomorphisms (see (

\scriptstyle{t^{2}_{A,X}}
\scriptstyle{t^{1}_{A,X}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{A\neq B,B^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B^{\prime}}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B,B^{\prime}),X(B^{\prime})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),X(B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\divideontimes}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B^{\prime}}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B^{\prime}),\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B^{\prime},B),X(B)))}

where
\divideontimes
denotes the composition of

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),X(B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\circ^{*}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B^{\prime},B)\otimes_{R_{B^{\prime}}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B^{\prime}),X(B))}

with the canonical isomorphism

\scalebox{0.8}{ \lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 102.56717pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-102.56717pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B^{\prime},B)\otimes_{R_{B^{\prime}}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B^{\prime}),X(B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 108.94217pt\raise 6.15pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\cong}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 126.56717pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 126.56717pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B^{\prime}}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B^{\prime}),\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B^{\prime},B),X(B)))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}.

Besides, consider the
R_{A}
-module homomorphism
\tau_{A,X}:=\prod_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}{\underline{X}_{BA}}_{*}

\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),X(B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{A,X}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B,A),X(A)))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),X(B))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\underline{X}_{BA}}_{*}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B,A),X(A)))}

It is easy to verify that the
R_{A}
-module homomorphism
\psi_{A}^{X}
is factorized over the kernel of
\mu_{A,X}:=(t^{1}_{A,X}-t^{2}_{A,X},\tau_{A,X})

\textstyle{X(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{A}^{X}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\prod_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),X(B))}
\textstyle{\ker(\mu_{A,X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

5.7. Proposition.

For any object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
,
({\rm c}_{A},{\rm s}_{A},{\rm k}_{A})
is an adjoint triple

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.66846pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.66846pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.09549pt\raise 25.00975pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm c}_{A}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 128.54529pt\raise 7.43953pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.77257pt\raise-25.93335pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.92778pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm k}_{A}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 129.52576pt\raise-7.00241pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 61.66846pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 91.66846pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 121.66846pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.28925pt\raise 5.00972pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm s}_{A}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.66847pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Proof.

We construct a natural isomorphism

\gamma:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.51625pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.51625pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}({\rm c}_{A}(-),-)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.51625pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 61.51625pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mbox{Nat}_{R}(-,{\rm s}_{A}(-))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Let
z\in\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}({\rm c}_{A}(X),M)
. We let
\gamma(z)_{A}:X(A)\longrightarrow M={\rm s}_{A}(M)(A)
be the composition of
z
with the canonical quotient homomorphism
X(A)\rightarrow{\rm c}_{A}(X)
, and
\gamma(z)_{B}:=0:X(B)\rightarrow 0={\rm s}_{A}(M)(B)
for
B\neq A
. Clearly,
\gamma(z)_{B}
is an
R
-module homomorphism for every object
B
in
\mathcal{A}
. Now we show that
{\gamma(z):=\{\gamma(z)_{B}\}_{B\in\mathcal{A}}}
is a natural transformation from
X
to
{\rm s}_{A}(M)
. For it, for a given morphism
{f:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}}
in
\mathcal{A}
, we need to show the commutativity of the following diagram

\textstyle{X(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X(f)}
\scriptstyle{\gamma(z)_{B}}
\textstyle{X(B^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma(z)_{B^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{{\rm s}_{A}(M)(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\rm s}_{A}(M)(f)}
\textstyle{{\rm s}_{A}(M)(B^{\prime})}

It is immediate whenever
B^{\prime}\neq A
. If
B=A=B^{\prime}
, then it follows from the fact that
\gamma(z)_{A}
is an
R_{A}
-module homomorphism. Now suppose that
B\neq A
and
B^{\prime}=A
.
X(f)
is factorized as

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.46701pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.46701pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.65967pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f\otimes\operatorname{id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.46701pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.46701pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{A}}XB\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 133.50636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 157.50635pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 157.50635pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}XB\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 316.33328pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 316.33328pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{XA}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Therefore,
\gamma(\alpha)_{A}\circ X(f)=0.
As a consequence,
\gamma:X\Rightarrow{\rm s}_{A}(M)
is a natural transformation. One can easily show the naturality and injectivity of
\gamma
.

Let
\alpha:X\Rightarrow{\rm s}_{A}(M)
be a natural transformation. As pointed out in (
4.1
),
\alpha_{A}
is an
R_{A}
-module homomorphism. Besides, for every
B\neq A
, the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}X(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{X}_{AB}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}\otimes\alpha_{B}}
\textstyle{X(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{A}}
\textstyle{0=\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}s_{A}(M)(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M}

So
\alpha_{A}
is factorized over
c_{A}(X)
, which shows the surjectivity of
\gamma
.

Hence,
({\rm c}_{A},{\rm s}_{A})
is an adjoint pair. Similar arguments can be applied for
({\rm s}_{A},{\rm k}_{A})
.

∎

We let

{\rm c},{\rm k}:\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\longrightarrow\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},

{{\rm s}:\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\longrightarrow\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})}

denote the functors
({\rm c}_{A})_{A\in\mathcal{A}},({\rm k}_{A})_{A\in\mathcal{A}}
and
\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm s}_{A}
, respectively.

5.8. Proposition.

({\rm c},{\rm s},{\rm k})
is an adjoint triple

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.66846pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.66846pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.74834pt\raise 24.50697pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm c}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 140.75659pt\raise 7.43953pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.42542pt\raise-25.43057pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm k}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 121.66187pt\raise-13.96507pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 61.66846pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 91.66846pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 121.66846pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.9421pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm s}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.66847pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Proof.

Note that for every object
B
in
\mathcal{A}
,

\bigoplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm s}_{A}(M_{A})(B)={\rm s}_{B}(M_{B})=\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm s}_{A}(M_{A})(B).

Therefore,

{\rm s}((M_{A})_{A\in\mathcal{A}})=\bigoplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm s}_{A}(M_{A})=\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm s}_{A}(M_{A}).

So we have

\begin{split}\operatorname{Hom}_{\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}}(({\rm c}_{A}(X))_{A\in\mathcal{A}},(M_{A})_{A\in\mathcal{A}})&:=\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}({\rm c}_{A}(X),M_{A})\\
&\cong\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,{\rm s}_{A}(M_{A}))\\
&\cong\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm s}_{A}(M_{A}))\\
\end{split}

Similarly,
({\rm s},{\rm k})
is proved to be an adjoint pair.
∎

5.9. Remark.

It is immediate that the functor
{\rm s}
is an exact functor from
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
to
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
.
However, neither
{\rm c}_{A}
nor
{\rm k}_{A}
preserve short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(-,{\rm s}_{A}(M))
and
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}({\rm s}_{A}(M),-)
, respectively, as required in Corollary
3.3
-(iii-iv). The category of chain complexes of left
R
-modules, which is a very particular case of
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
, can be considered as a counterexample (see Example
3.6
). For a more general case, for example, assume that there exist two different objects
A
and
B
in
\mathcal{A}
such that
\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)
is a flat right
R_{A}
-module. For any non-trivial left
R_{A}
-module
M
, consider the short exact sequence

\textstyle{K_{M,A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm q}_{A}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm s}_{A}(M)}

given in Proposition
5.14
. Note that
the associated
R_{A}
-module homomorphism

\underline{{\rm q}_{A}(M)}_{AB}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.74303pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.74303pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm q}_{A}(M)(A)\cong M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.74303pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.74303pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(\operatorname{Hom}(A,B),{\rm q}_{A}(M)(B))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

{\rm q}_{A}(M)(A)(m)=-\otimes m:\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\otimes_{R_{A}}M
, is injective, and therefore,
{\rm k}_{A}({\rm q}_{A}(M))=0
. Applying
{\rm k}_{A}
to the aforementioned short exact exact sequence, we have
0\rightarrow 0\rightarrow M
.

Applying Corollary
3.3
to the adjoint triples given in Proposition
5.7
and Proposition
5.8
, we obtain the following two corollaries.

5.10. Corollary.

Let
A
be an object in
\mathcal{A}
. For a given cotorsion pair
(\mathcal{F}_{A},\mathcal{G}_{A})
in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, the following statements hold:

(i)

If
\mathcal{F}_{A}
is a generating class in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then

{\rm s}_{A}(\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}=\biggl\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm k}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{G}_{A}\textrm{ and }\\
$X$\textrm{ is a }{\rm k}_{A}\textrm{-coflat object}\end{array}\biggr\}.

(ii)

If
\mathcal{G}_{A}
is a cogenerating class in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm s}_{A}(\mathcal{G}_{A})=\biggl\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm c}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{F}_{A}\textrm{ and }\\
$X$\textrm{ is a }{\rm c}_{A}\textrm{-flat object}\end{array}\biggr\}.

5.11. Corollary.

For any cotorsion pair
(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, the following statements hold:

(i)

If
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A}
is a generating class in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then

{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}=\biggl\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm k}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{G}_{A}\textrm{ and }$X$\textrm{ is a }{\rm k}_{A}\textrm{-coflat}\\
\textrm{object for every }A\in\mathcal{A}\end{array}\biggr\}.

(ii)

If
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A}
is a cogenerating class in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})=\biggl\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm c}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{F}_{A}\textrm{ and }$X$\textrm{ is a }{\rm c}_{A}\textrm{-flat}\\
\textrm{object for every }A\in\mathcal{A}\end{array}\biggr\}.

5.12. Remark.

From Lemma
2.1
-(i-ii), it is immediate that
{\rm p}_{B}(G)\in{\rm s}_{A}(\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}
, and
{\rm q}_{B}(F)\in{}^{\perp}{\rm s}_{A}(\mathcal{G}_{A})
for every
A,B\in\mathcal{A}
,
F\in\mathcal{F}_{B}
and
G\in\mathcal{G}_{B}
. By Proposition
4.11
, if
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A}
is a cogenerating class in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, so is
{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}
; if
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A}
is a generating class in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, so is
{}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
.

5.13. Remark.

If we consider the absolute exact structure on both categories

\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
and
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then by Proposition
2.10
and Proposition
2.15
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\mid\ {\rm k}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{G}_{A}\textrm{ and ${\rm R^{1}k}_{A}(X)=0$ for every $A\in\mathcal{A}$}\},

{}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})

\displaystyle=\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\mid\ {\rm c}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{F}_{A}\textrm{ and ${\rm L_{1}c}_{A}(X)=0$ for every $A\in\mathcal{A}$}\}

which are equal to the classes
\Psi(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
and
\Phi(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})
, respectively, introduced in
[
HJ19
, Definition 1.5,Theorem 1.7]
.

The notion of (co)flatness related to a functor is, in general, abstract and not easily visualized. To better understand
{\rm c}_{A}
-flat and
{\rm k}_{A}
-coflat objects in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
, we aim to describe them in terms of specific short exact sequences, similar to the one given in Example
3.6
. To do this, we first need to prove the following auxiliary results.

5.14. Lemma.

For every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
and a left
R_{A}
-module
M
, the canonical natural transformations

{\rm q}_{A}(M)\Longrightarrow{\rm s}_{A}(M)\quad\textrm{ and }\quad{\rm s}_{A}(M)\Longrightarrow{\rm p}_{A}(M)

are admissible epic and admissible monic, respectively, in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
.

Proof.

We only prove that the first morphism is an admissible epic as the argument for the second one is similar. Let
\alpha:{\rm q}_{A}(M)\Rightarrow{\rm s}_{A}(M)
be the counit of the adjunction
({\rm q_{A}},{\rm ev_{A}})
for the functor
{\rm s}_{A}(M)
. It is easy to verify that

\alpha_{A}:R_{A}\otimes_{R_{A}}M\rightarrow M

is the canonical isomorphism induced from the left scalar multiplication of
M
by elements in
R_{A}
, and
\alpha_{B}=0:\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\otimes_{R_{A}}M\rightarrow 0
for
B\neq A
. So for every object
B
in
\mathcal{A}
, the morphism
\alpha_{B}
is an admissible epic in
\mathcal{E}_{B}
. By construction of
\mathcal{E}
(see (
4.10
)),
\alpha
is an admissible epic in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
.
∎

5.15. Remark.

The canonical morphisms given in Lemma
5.14
induce the following canonical short exact sequences in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})

\textstyle{{\rm K}_{M,A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm q}_{A}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm s}_{A}(M)}

\textstyle{{\rm s}_{A}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm p}_{A}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm C}_{M,A}}

From the construction, it is immediate that the functors
{\rm K}_{M,A},{\rm C}_{M,A}:\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}
satisfies

{\rm K}_{M,A}(B):=\begin{cases}0&;B=A\\
{\rm q}_{A}(M)(B)&;B\neq A\end{cases}

{\rm C}_{M,A}(B):=\begin{cases}0&;B=A\\
{\rm p}_{A}(M)(B)&;B\neq A\end{cases}

5.16. Lemma.

For any functor
X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
and a left
R_{A}
-module
M
, we have

\displaystyle\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,{\rm C}_{M,A})

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X}),M);

\displaystyle\mbox{Nat}_{R}({\rm K}_{M,A},X)

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(M,\ker(\mu_{A,X})).

Proof.

Due to the duality of arguments, we only prove the isomorphism

\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,{\rm C}_{M,A})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X}),M)
. Let
\alpha:X\Rightarrow{\rm C}_{M,A}
be a natural transformation. From (
4.1
), we already know that
\alpha_{B}
is an
R_{B}
-module homomorphism for any object
B
. Now, for a given object
B
in
\mathcal{A}
with
B\neq A
, we let
\overline{\alpha}_{B}
denote the
R_{A}
-module homomorphisms in
\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}X(B),M)\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(X(B),{\rm p}_{A}(M)(B))
corresponding to
\alpha_{B}
under the usual adjunction. Note that
\overline{\alpha}_{B}(f\otimes x)=\alpha_{B}(x)(f)
. It is easy to verify that for every two objects
B,B^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}
different from
A
, we have

\overline{\alpha}_{B^{\prime}}\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\overline{X}_{BB^{\prime}})=\overline{\alpha}_{B}\circ(\circ\otimes\operatorname{id})

as given in the diagram (
5.6.2
), and since
\alpha
is a natural transformation,
{\alpha_{B}\circ X(l)=0}
for every morphism
l:A\rightarrow B
, and hence,
\overline{\alpha}_{B}\circ(\operatorname{id}\otimes\overline{X}_{AB})=0
(see the diagram (
5.6.2
)). So there exists a unique
R_{A}
-module homomorphism
z_{\alpha}:\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\longrightarrow M
satisfying

z_{\alpha}\circ\sigma_{A}=\Sigma_{A\neq B}\overline{\alpha}_{B}:\displaystyle\bigoplus_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}X(B)\longrightarrow M.

Clearly, the assignment

\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,{\rm C}_{M,A})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X}),M),\quad\alpha\rightsquigarrow z_{\alpha},

is well defined, and is natural in both
X
and
M
. Since
\sigma_{A}
is an epimorphism, it is also injective.

Now, let
z:\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\longrightarrow M
be an
R_{A}
-module homomorphism. We let
\alpha_{A}:=0:X(A)\longrightarrow 0
, and for an object
B\neq A
, we let
\alpha_{B}
denote the
R_{B}
-module homomorphism in
\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(X(B),{\rm p}_{A}(M)(B))\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}X(B),M)
corresponding to the
R_{A}
-module homomorphism
z\circ\sigma_{A}
restricted to
\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}X(B)
under the usual adjunction. Note that
\alpha_{B}(x)=z\circ\sigma_{A}(-\otimes x).
By restriction of scalars, the family
{\alpha:=\{\alpha_{B}\}_{B\in\mathcal{A}}}
is clearly a family of
R
-module homomorphisms. We claim that
\alpha
is a natural transformation from
X
to
{\rm C}_{M,A}
. For it, consider a morphism
f:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}
. The case when
B^{\prime}=A
is immediate, as
\alpha_{A}\circ X(f)=0={\rm C}_{M,A}(f)\circ\alpha_{B}
. Now suppose that
B^{\prime}\neq A
. Then there are two cases to consider:
B\neq A
and
B=A
.

B\neq A
: For any morphism
g:B^{\prime}\longrightarrow A
and
x\in X(B)
, we have

\displaystyle({\rm C}_{M,A}(f)\circ\alpha_{B})(x)(g)

\displaystyle=(z\circ\sigma_{A})((g\circ f)\otimes x)=(z\circ\sigma_{A})(g\otimes X(f)(x))

\displaystyle=\alpha_{B^{\prime}}(X(f)(x))(g)=(\alpha_{B^{\prime}}\circ X(f))(x)(g),

which implies
{\rm C}_{M,A}(f)\circ\alpha_{B}=\alpha_{B^{\prime}}\circ X(f)
.

B=A
: For any object
x
in
X(A)
, we have

(\alpha_{B^{\prime}}\circ X(f))(x)(-)=z\circ\sigma_{A}(-\otimes X(f)(x)):\operatorname{Hom}(B^{\prime},A)\longrightarrow M

But
\sigma_{A}(g\otimes X(f)(x))=0
for any morphism
g\in\operatorname{Hom}(B^{\prime},A)
. So

\alpha_{B^{\prime}}\circ X(f)=0={\rm C}_{M,A}(f)\circ\alpha_{A}.

As a consequence,
\alpha
is a natural transformation, and therefore, the assignment
\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,{\rm C}_{M,A})\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\phi_{A,X}),M)
defined above is surjective.
∎

5.17. Lemma.

For every two objects
A\neq B
in
\mathcal{A}
,

(i)

{\rm c}_{A}\circ{\rm q}_{A}=\operatorname{id}
and
{\rm c}_{A}\circ{\rm q}_{B}=0
;

(ii)

{\rm k}_{A}\circ{\rm p}_{A}=\operatorname{id}
and
{\rm k}_{A}\circ{\rm p}_{B}=0
.

Proof.

By adjunctions, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}({\rm c}_{A}({\rm q}_{A}(-)),-)

\displaystyle\cong\mbox{Nat}_{R}({\rm q}_{A}(-),{\rm s}_{A}(-))

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(-,{\rm ev}_{A}({\rm s}_{A}(-)))

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(-,\operatorname{id}(-)).

\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}({\rm c}_{A}({\rm q}_{B}(-)),-)\cong\mbox{Nat}_{R}({\rm q}_{B}(-),{\rm s}_{A}(-))\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{B}}(-,{\rm ev}_{B}({\rm s}_{A}(-)))=0,

So
{\rm c}_{A}\circ{\rm q}_{A}=\operatorname{id}
and
{\rm c}_{A}\circ{\rm q}_{B}=0
. By similar arguments, we have the equalities given in (ii).
∎

Now we can provide a concrete description of
{\rm c}_{A}
-flat and
{\rm k}_{A}
-coflat functors, which will also be crucial in proving Theorem
6.5
. Recall morphisms
\phi_{A,X}
and
\ker(\mu_{A,X})
introduced in (
5.6
).

5.18. Proposition.

Let
A
be an object in
\mathcal{A}
. For any additive functor
{X:\mathcal{A}\longrightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}}
, the following statements hold.

(i)

X
is
{\rm c}_{A}
-flat in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
if and only if the canonical sequence

\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\longrightarrow X(A)\longrightarrow{\rm c}_{A}(X)

given in (
5.6
) is a short exact sequence in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
.

(ii)

X
is
{\rm k}_{A}
-coflat in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
if and only if the canonical sequence

{\rm k}_{A}(X)\longrightarrow X(A)\longrightarrow\ker(\mu_{A,X})

given in (
5.6
) is a short exact sequence in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
.

Proof.

We only prove the first statement.

(\Rightarrow)
Suppose that
X
is a
{\rm c}_{A}
-flat object in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
. We firstly show that the morphism

X(A)\rightarrow{\rm c}_{A}(X)
is an admissible epic in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
. For it, using the identity morphism
\operatorname{id}_{X(B)}
for every object
B
in
\mathcal{A}
and the adjoint pair
({\rm q},({\rm ev}_{B})_{B\in\mathcal{A}})
, we have the canonical natural transformation

\alpha:\bigoplus_{B\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{B}(X(B))\Rightarrow X,

which is an admissible epic in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
just as pointed out in the proof of Proposition
4.11
. By assumption,
{\rm c}_{A}(\alpha)
is an admissible epic in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
. As
{\rm c}_{A}
is a left adjoint functor, it preserves coproducts, and using Lemma
5.17
-(i), we have

{\rm c}_{A}(\bigoplus_{B\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{B}(X(B)))\cong\bigoplus_{B\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm c}_{A}({\rm q}_{B}(X(B))\cong X(A).

So
{\rm c}_{A}(\alpha)
is isomorphic to the canonical quotient morphism
X(A)\rightarrow{\rm c}_{A}(X)
. In other words, it is an admissible epic in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
.

On the other hand, let
E
be an injective cogenerator left
R_{A}
-module in the absolute sense ( that is, an injective cogenerating object in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
regarding to the absolute exact structure). It is clearly an injective object with respect to the exact structure
\mathcal{E}_{A}
, as well. Consider the following canonical short exact sequence in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
as given in Lemma
5.14

\mathbb{E}:\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.09723pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.09723pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm s}_{A}(E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 16.09724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.09723pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.09723pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm p}_{A}(E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 97.90283pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 97.90283pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm C}_{E,A}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Applying the functor
\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,-)
, it induces the following long exact sequence of abelian groups

\scalebox{0.75}{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,{\rm s}_{A}(E))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 127.71295pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,{\rm p}_{A}(E))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 227.53702pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 227.53702pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise C}_{E,A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 321.8111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 321.8111pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(X,{\rm s}_{A}(E))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 418.9223pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 418.9223pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(X,{\rm p}_{A}(E))}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}.

Since
{\rm ev}_{A}
is an exact functor,
{\rm p}_{A}(E)
is an injective object in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
. So
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(X,{\rm p}_{A}(E))=0
.
Besides, as
X
is a
{\rm c}_{A}
-flat object in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
, by Proposition
2.10
, we have

\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathcal{E}}^{1}(X,{\rm s}_{A}(E))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\mathcal{E}_{A}}({\rm c}_{A}(X),E)=0
, and therefore,
\mbox{Nat}_{R}(X,\mathbb{E})
is a short exact sequence of abelian groups. By adjunctions and Lemma
5.16
, it is isomorphic to

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}({\rm c}_{A}(X),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(X(A),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{R_{A}}(\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X}),E)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

.

As
E
is a cogenerating left
R_{A}
-module,
\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})
is the kernel of the admissible epic
X(A)\rightarrow{\rm c}_{A}(X)
, and therefore,

\textstyle{\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm c}_{A}(X)}

is a short exact sequence in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
.

(\Leftarrow)
Suppose that the canonical sequence

\textstyle{\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm c}_{A}(X)}

is a short exact sequence in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
. By Lemma
2.8
-(i), it is enough to show that
{\rm c}_{A}
preserves short exact sequences in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}(X,{\rm s}_{A}(-))
. Let
\mathbb{E}
be a short exact sequence in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
of the form

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.5139pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.5139pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm s}_{A}(M)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.51392pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.5139pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.5139pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.45618pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\alpha}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.05556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 79.05556pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
M
is a left
R_{A}
-module.
Without lost of generality, we can assume that
X(B)=Z(B)
for every object
B
in
\mathcal{A}
with
A\neq B
. So we have
h^{1}_{A,X}=h^{1}_{A,Z}
and
h^{2}_{A,X}=h^{2}_{A,Z}
. On the other hand, we have the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\otimes_{R_{A}}Z(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\oplus\operatorname{id}\otimes\operatorname{id}\otimes\alpha_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{A,Z}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}\operatorname{Hom}(A,B)\otimes_{R_{A}}X(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{A,X}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}Z(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}X(B)}

whose upper horizontal arrow is an epimorphism. So the images of
\nu_{A,X}
and
\nu_{A,Z}
coincide, and therefore, we have
\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})=\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,Z})

together with the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}Z(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{A}^{Z}}
\textstyle{\displaystyle\bigoplus_{A\neq B\in\mathcal{A}}\operatorname{Hom}(B,A)\otimes_{R_{B}}X(B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{A}^{X}}
\textstyle{\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X(A)}

By
[
Buh10
, Proposition 2.16]
, the canonical morphism
\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,Z})\longrightarrow Z(A)
is an admissible monic in
R_{A}
, as well. Applying Snake Lemma (see
[
Buh10
, Corollary 8.13]
) to the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Z(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X(A)}

we obtain the following short exact sequence in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.55557pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.55557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M={\rm c}_{A}({\rm s}_{A}(M))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.55557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.55557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.55557pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm c}_{A}(Z)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 117.8334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 117.8334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\rm c}_{A}(X)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

∎

5.19. Theorem.

For any cotorsion pair
(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})
in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, the following statements hold.

(i)

If
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A}
is a generating class in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then

{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{F}_{A})^{\perp}=\biggl\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm k}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{G}_{A},X(A)\longrightarrow\ker(\mu_{A,X})\\
\textrm{is an admissible epic in }R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},\ \forall A\in\mathcal{A}\end{array}\biggr\}.

(ii)

If
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A}
is a cogenerating class in
\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
, then

{}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{G}_{A})=\biggl\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm c}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{F}_{A},\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\hookrightarrow X(A)\\
\textrm{is an admissible monic in }R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},\ \forall A\in\mathcal{A}\end{array}\biggr\}.

6.
Projective and injective functors

In this section, we apply Theorem
5.19
to provide an intrinsic characterization of projective and injective functors. We retain all the hypotheses assumed on
\mathcal{A}
so far. Applying Theorem
5.19
-(i) to the trivial cotorsion pair
(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}_{A}\mbox{-Inj})
, we have induces

{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod})^{\perp}=\left\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm k}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{E}_{A}\mbox{-Inj},\\
X(A)\longrightarrow\ker(\mu_{A,X})\textrm{ is an admissible}\\
\textrm{epic in }R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},\ \forall A\in\mathcal{A}\end{array}\right\}.

Similarly, by Theorem
5.19
-(ii), the trivial cotorsion pair
(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{E}_{A}\mbox{-Proj},\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod})
leads to

{}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod})=\left\{X:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow R\mbox{-Mod}\biggm|\ \begin{array}[]{l}{\rm c}_{A}(X)\in\mathcal{E}_{A}\mbox{-Proj},\\
\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\hookrightarrow X(A)\textrm{ is an admissible}\\
\textrm{monic in }R_{A}\mbox{-Mod},\ \forall A\in\mathcal{A}\end{array}\right\}.

The following containments in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
can be easily verified

\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Inj}\subseteq{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod})^{\perp}\quad\textrm{ and }\quad\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Proj}\subseteq{}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}).

The classes on the right side of the above inclusions have a precise description and are easier to work from a homological point of view. Furthermore, in the very well-known cases of functor categories (e.g. the category of chain complexes of left
R
-modules, the module category over a formal triangular matrix ring etc.), they give rise to injective and projective objects in functor categories. We need to impose further conditions on
\mathcal{A}
to obtain the reverse containments.

6.1. Condition.

There doesn’t exist a chain of morphisms in
\mathcal{A}

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{1}}

satisfying
A_{i}\neq A_{j}
for every
1\leq i\neq j
, and
f_{1}\circ\cdots\circ f_{n}\neq 0
for every
n\geq 1
.

6.2. Condition.

There doesn’t exist a chain of morphisms in
\mathcal{A}

\textstyle{A_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{A_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{3}}
\textstyle{\cdots}

satisfying
A_{i}\neq A_{j}
for every
1\leq i\neq j
, and
f_{n}\circ\cdots\circ f_{1}\neq 0
for every
n\geq 1
.

6.3. Lemma.

For any functor
X\in\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
, the following statements hold.

(i)

If Condition
6.1
is satisfied, then
X=0
if and only if
{\rm c}_{A}(X)=0
for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
.

(ii)

If Condition
6.2
is satisfied, then
X=0
if and only if
{\rm k}_{A}(X)=0
for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
.

Proof.

The necessity part is clear. Now suppose that
{\rm c}_{A}(X)=0
for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
. It implies that the
R_{A}
-module homomorphism
\varphi_{A}^{X}
is an epimorphism for every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
. For the sake of contradiction, we assume that
X\neq 0
. Consider an object
A_{1}
in
\mathcal{A}
such that
X(A_{1})\neq 0
. Let
x_{1}
be a non-zero object in
X(A_{1})
.
By assumption, there exist a finite set
I_{2}
of objects in
\mathcal{A}
different from
A_{1}
, and a family
\{(f_{B},x_{B})\}_{B\in I_{2}}
, where
f_{B}\in\operatorname{Hom}(B,A_{1})
and
x_{B}\in X(B)
, such that

\sum_{B\in I_{2}}X(f_{B})(x_{B})=x_{1}.

Without lost of generality, we can assume that
X(f_{B})(x_{B})\neq 0
for every
B\in I_{2}
. We choose any object object in
I_{2}
, and we denote it by
A_{2}
, and
f_{1}:=f_{A_{2}}
,
x_{2}:=x_{A_{2}}
. Similarly, as
x_{2}\neq 0
, there exist a finite family
I_{3}
of objects in
\mathcal{A}
different from
A_{2}
, and a family
\{(f_{B},x_{B})\}_{B\in I_{3}}
, where
f_{B}\in\operatorname{Hom}(B,A_{2})
and
x_{B}\in X(B)
, such that

\sum_{B\in I_{3}}X(f_{B})(x_{B})=x_{2}.

We claim that there is an object
B_{0}\in I_{3}
such that

X(f_{1}\circ f_{B_{0}})(x_{B_{0}})=(X(f_{1})\circ X(f_{B_{0}}))(x_{B_{0}})\neq 0.

Indeed, if
X(f_{1}\circ f_{B})(x_{B})=0
for every
B\in I_{3}
, then we would have

X(f_{1})(x_{2})=X(f_{1})(\sum_{B\in I_{3}}X(f_{B})(x_{B}))=\sum_{B\in I_{3}}X(f_{1}\circ f_{B})(x_{B})=0,

which is a contradiction. We let
A_{3}
be any object in
I_{3}
such that

(X(f_{1}\circ f_{A_{3}}))(x_{A_{3}})\neq 0.

We let
f_{2}:=f_{A_{3}}
and
x_{3}:=x_{A_{3}}
. Note that
A_{3}\neq A_{2}
. From Hypothesis
5.1
, we also have
A_{3}\neq A_{1}
.

By induction, we can construct a sequence of morphisms in
\mathcal{A}

\textstyle{\cdots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{2}}
\textstyle{A_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{A_{1}}

with
A_{i}\neq A_{j}
for every
i\neq j\geq 1
such that
X(f_{1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{n})\neq 0
, and therefore,

f_{1}\circ\ldots\circ f_{n}\neq 0
for every
n\geq 1
, which is a contradiction by assumption. So
X=0
.
∎

The previous lemma is slightly different and more general than the result given in
[
HJ22
, Theorem 7.19]
as we have the following example

6.4. Example.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a category with objects
\mathbb{N}
and morphisms

\operatorname{Hom}(n,m):=\begin{cases}R&n\in\{m,\ldots,2m\}\\
0&\textrm{otherwise}\end{cases}

It clearly satisfies Condition
6.1
, but it is not left rooted nor fulfills the conditions given in
[
HJ22
, Theorem 7.29]
.

Following a similar strategy used in the proof of
[
HJ22
, Theorem 7.29]
, we have the follwing characterization of projective and injective additive functors.

6.5. Theorem.

(i)

If Condition
6.1
is satisfied, then

\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Proj}={}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}).

(ii)

If Condition
6.2
is satisfied, then

\mathcal{E}\mbox{-Inj}={\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod})^{\perp}

Proof.

We only prove the first statement. We already know the inclusion
\subseteq
. Now, let
X\in{}^{\perp}{\rm s}(\prod_{A\in\mathcal{A}}R_{A}\mbox{-Mod})
. Then for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
, we have the following canonical short exact sequence

(6.5.1)

\textstyle{\mbox{coker}(\phi_{A,X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{XA\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{A}}
\textstyle{{\rm c}_{A}(X)}

with an
\mathcal{E}_{A}
-projective left
R_{A}
-module
{\rm c}_{A}(X)
. So for every
A\in\mathcal{A}
, there exists an admissible monic
\iota_{A}:{\rm c}_{A}(X)\hookrightarrow X(A)
such that
\pi_{A}\circ\iota_{A}=\operatorname{id}
.
Using the counit of the adjunction
({\rm q},({\rm ev}_{A})_{A\in\mathcal{A}})
, we obtain a natural transformation
\alpha:\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}({\rm c}_{A}(X))\Rightarrow X
. Note that
\alpha
is the composition of the splitting admissible monic
\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}(\iota_{A}):\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}({\rm c}_{A}(X))\Rightarrow\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}(X(A))
with the canonical natural transformation

\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}(X(A))\Rightarrow X,

which is an admissible epic in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
as pointed out in the proof of Proposition
4.11
. Note that
\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}({\rm c}_{A}(X))
is an
\mathcal{E}
-projective object in
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})

By Lemma
5.17
, for any object
B
in
\mathcal{A}
, we have

{\rm c}_{B}(\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm q}_{A}({\rm c}_{A}(X)))\cong\oplus_{A\in\mathcal{A}}{\rm c}_{B}({\rm q}_{A}({\rm c}_{A}(X))\cong{\rm c}_{A}(X),

and therefore,
{\rm c}_{B}(X)
is an isomorphism. Since

{\rm c}_{B}
preserves all colimits,
{\rm c}_{B}(\mbox{coker}(\alpha))=\mbox{coker}({\rm c}_{B}(\alpha))=0
for every object
B
in
\mathcal{A}
. By Lemma
6.3
-(i),
\mbox{coker}(\alpha)=0
, that is,
\alpha
is an epimorphism.

Notice that as
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
is an abelian category, the short exact sequence
6.5.1
in
(R_{A}\mbox{-Mod};\mathcal{E}_{A})
is a usual short exact sequence in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
with respect to the absolute exact structure. So by Proposition
5.18
, for every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
,
X
is a
{\rm c}_{A}
-flat object whenever both categories
\mbox{Add}(\mathcal{A},R\mbox{-Mod})
and
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
are considered to be equipped with the absolute exact structures. It implies that

{\rm c}_{A}(\ker(\alpha))=\ker({\rm c}_{A}(\alpha))=0

for every object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, and hence, by Lemma
6.3
-(i),
\ker(\alpha)=0
. As a consecuence,
\alpha
is an isomorphism.
∎

6.6. Remark.

If one starts with an exact structure
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
on
R\mbox{-Mod}
, then by restriction of scalars, it leads to a family
\{\mathcal{E}_{A}\}_{A\in\mathcal{A}}
of exact structures on
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
defined as follows: a short exact sequence
\mathbb{E}_{A}
in
R_{A}\mbox{-Mod}
belongs to
\mathcal{E}_{A}
if and only if it belongs to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
as a short exact sequence of
R
-modules. Note that
\mathcal{E}_{A}
-projective and injective objects are of the form
R_{A}\otimes_{R}M
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(R_{A},N)
, respectively, where
M\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\mbox{-Proj}
and
N\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\mbox{-Inj}
.
